Topological exodromy with coefficients
Mauro PORTA, Jean-Baptiste Teyssier

Abstract
. We improve the exodromy equivalence of MacPherson, Treumann and Lurie in several ways: first, we allow stratified spaces that have locally weakly contractible strata, rather than being locally of singular shape, we remove all noetherianity assumptions and we consider more general coefficients (e.g. compactly assembled or stable presentable ∞ \infty -categories). Furthermore, our approach shows that the exodromy equivalence is functorial for every morphism of stratified spaces. As an application, we construct, under suitable finiteness assumptions, a higher Artin derived stack of hyperconstructible hypersheaves and prove that every perversity function gives rise to an open substack of perverse hypersheaves. Using the derived structure, we provide the construction of a new cohomological Hall algebra, generalizing the ones of character varieties studied in the past by Schiffmann-Vasserot, Davison and Mistry.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
55P65,55P65,55U25,14D23,32S40,32S60

1.
Introduction

Let
X
be a locally contractible topological space.
In classical terms, the monodromy correspondence is an equivalence between the abelian category
\mathrm{LC}(X;\mathrm{Ab})
of locally constant sheaves of abelian groups on
X
and the abelian category
\Fun(\Pi_{1}(X),\mathrm{Ab})
of representations of the first fundamental groupoid of
X
.
This equivalence can be seen as a concrete way of bridging the topological world, incarnated by the locally constant sheaves, and the algebraic world, incarnated by the representations of the first fundamental groupoid of
X
.
The advent of higher categorical techniques
[
HTT
]
allowed to improve this correspondence by replacing
\Pi_{1}(X)
with the
homotopy type

\Pi_{\infty}(X)
of
X
, which in many senses is still an object of algebraic nature.
There is a natural map
\Pi_{\infty}(X)\to\Pi_{1}(X)
, which induces an equivalence

\Fun(\Pi_{1}(X),\mathrm{Ab})\longrightarrow\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}(X),\mathrm{Ab})\ ,

but interestingly this is no longer true if we replace
\mathrm{Ab}
by its derived
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Ab})
1
1

1

Notice that throughout the main body of the paper, we will rather write
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
instead of
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Ab})
.
.
On the other hand, what is still true is that there is a canonical equivalence

\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}(X),\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Ab}))\simeq\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Ab}))\ ,

where the right hand side denotes the
\infty
-category of hypersheaves that are locally the hypersheafification of a constant presheaf.
Notice that these two categories are sensitive to the whole homotopy type of
X
, rather than just to its first fundamental groupoid.

There is a second natural generalization of the monodromy correspondence, whose fundamental idea is due to MacPherson.
When
X
is equipped with a stratification
P
, that is a continuous morphism
X\to P
where
P
is a poset endowed with the Alexandroff topology (cf.
Section

2.1
), one can introduce a stratified variant of
\Pi_{1}(X)
, noted
\Pi_{1}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
.
This is a non-full subcategory of
\Pi_{1}(X)
which is no longer a groupoid; its objects are the same (that is, the points of
X
), but the only morphisms that are allowed are those that
exit
from lower strata to go to upper ones.
For this reason, the category
\Pi_{1}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
is called the
category of exit paths of
(X,P)
.
In
[
T
, Theorem 5.7]
, D. Treumann studied this category and established an equivalence

\Fun\big(\Pi_{1}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathrm{Ab}\big)\simeq\Cons_{P}(X;\mathrm{Ab})\ ,

where the right hand side denotes the abelian category of
\mathrm{Ab}
-valued constructible sheaves.
Following the influential work
[
BGH
]
, this equivalence is nowadays referred to as the
exodromy equivalence
.
It was generalized to the higher categorical world by J. Lurie in
[
HA
, Appendix A]
.
His first step is to define a version
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
of
\Pi_{1}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
and establish a major result (Theorem A.6.4 in
loc. cit.
) asserting that if the stratification
(X,P)
is
conical
, then
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
is indeed an
\infty
-category.
Then, he proves:

Theorem 1.1

(J. Lurie,
[
HA
, Theorem A.9.3]
)
.

Let
X
be a paracompact topological space which is locally of singular shape and equipped with a conical
P
-stratification, where
P
is a partially ordered set satisfying the ascending chain condition.
Then there is a natural equivalence

\Psi_{X,P}\colon\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{S}\big)\longrightarrow\Cons_{P}(X;\mathcal{S})\ ,

where
\mathcal{S}
denotes the
\infty
-category of homotopy types (a.k.a.
\infty
-groupoids or animated sets).

The goal of this paper is to improve this result in several ways:

(1)

replace the condition that
X
is locally of singular shape with the weaker condition that the strata of
(X,P)
are locally weakly contractible;

(2)

remove the ascending chain condition on the poset
P
;

(3)

allow more general coefficients than
\mathcal{S}
; in particular every presentable
\infty
-category that is either stable or a retract of a compactly generated
\infty
-category is permitted.

(4)

provide an explicit formula for the inverse of
\Psi_{X,P}
.

We refer to
5.17
for the precise statement. Let us observe that improvements (1) and (2) are related, and the latter has already been addressed by D. Lejay in
[
L
]
.
The key idea is to replace the
\infty
-category
\Cons_{P}(X;\mathcal{S})
with the
\infty
-category
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{S})
of
P
-
hyper
constructible
hyper
sheaves.
Indeed, the singular shape assumption guarantees that every locally constant sheaf is automatically a hypersheaf.
Working with hypersheaves from the very beginning, we can relax this assumption and replace it with the weaker one of having locally weakly contractible strata.

Improvements (3) and (4) are also related.
The proof contained in
[
HA
]
explicitly relies on model categories, and is heavily built on the fact that functors from
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
to
\mathcal{S}
can be realized as left fibrations over
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
.
These two facts together make it difficult to adapt the proof
verbatim
to different coefficients than
\mathcal{S}
.
On the other hand, it is also difficult to bootstrap on Lurie’s result to deduce the same result for arbitrary presentable
\infty
-categories, although it would be possible to deal with the case of compactly generated
\infty
-categories in this way.

We propose therefore a new proof which is purely
\infty
-categorical and that we briefly summarize below.
Our approach has also several pleasant consequences.
First and foremost, we obtain a
better functoriality
of the exodromy equivalence.
Notably, if
f\colon(X,P)\to(Y,Q)
is a morphism between conically stratified spaces, then the square

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(Y,Q),\mathcal{S}\big)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{Q}(Y;\mathcal{S})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{S}\big)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P}(X;\mathcal{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{Y,Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{X,P}}

is made
2
-commutative by a natural transformation
\psi_{f}
that is a priori not invertible.
We say that
f
is
exodromic
if
\psi_{f}
is an equivalence.
In
[
HA
, Proposition A.9.6]
it is shown that if the stratification on
X
coincides with the stratification
f^{-1}(Q)
induced by
f
, then
f
is exodromic.
Our approach allows to improve this result:

Corollary 1.2

(See
6.8
)
.

If
f\colon(X,P)\to(Y,Q)
is a morphism between conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata, then
f
is exodromic.

As a consequence of this improved functoriality, we obtain the following:

Corollary 1.3

(See
6.12
)
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
(X,Q)
be a conical refinement of
P
.
Assume that the strata of
(X,P)
and
(X,Q)
are locally weakly contractible.
Then the natural map

f\colon\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q)\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)

is a localization.

Taking
P
to be the trivial stratification, we deduce that
\Pi_{\infty}(X)
is a localization of
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q)
.
This result, which is of course extremely intuitive at the geometric level, was only known to the best of our knowledge in the conically smooth situation
[
AFTb
, Proposition 1.2.13]
.

The key new ingredient in our proof is to interpolate between
\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}
and
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
as follows.
Given a conically stratified space
(X,P)
, let
\mathrm{E}_{X}
be the
\infty
-category informally defined as:

•

its objects are pairs
(U,x)
, where
U
is an open subset of
X
and
x
is an object in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)
, where
U
is seen as a
P
-stratified space in the natural way;

•

given two objects
(U,x)
and
(V,y)
, the mapping space
\Map_{\mathrm{E}_{X}}((U,x),(V,y))
is non-empty if and only if
V\subset U
, and in that case it coincides with
\Map_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)}(x,y)
.

Put otherwise,
\mathrm{E}_{X}
is the Grothendieck construction of the functor
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}\colon\mathrm{Open}(X)\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
We refer to
Section

3
and to
5.3
for the precise definition of
\mathrm{E}_{X}
.
This
\infty
-category comes with natural functors

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{X}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{X}}

Then the functor
\Psi_{X,P}
of Lurie can be identified with
\pi_{X,\ast}\circ\lambda_{X}^{\ast}
where
\pi_{X,\ast}
denotes the right Kan extension along
\pi_{X,\ast}
, and
\lambda_{X}^{\ast}
denotes the restriction along
\lambda_{X}
.
The main bulk of the paper is dedicated to show, for more general coefficients than just
\mathcal{S}
and independently from Lurie’s result, that this functor and its left adjoint
\Phi_{X,P}\coloneqq\lambda_{X,!}\circ\pi_{X}^{\ast}
realize the exodromy equivalence.

Let
F\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
be a hypersheaf.
A simple inspection reveals that
\Phi_{X,P}(F)
evaluated at one point
x\in X
seen as an object in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
can be written as

\Phi_{X,P}(F)(x)\simeq\colim_{(U,y,\gamma)}F(U)\ ,

where the colimit ranges over all morphisms
\gamma\colon y\to x
in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
and over all possible open neighborhood
U
of
y
.
If we could simply limit ourselves to the case where
\gamma
is the identity of
x
, this would simply produce the stalk
F_{x}
of
F
at the point
x
.
However, this is typically false and
\Phi_{X,P}(F)(x)
should be rather thought of as an “average over all the possible stalks at points close to
x
”.
Remarkably, when
F
is
P
-hyperconstructible, we can prove that there is indeed an equivalence

\Phi_{X,P}(F)(x)\simeq F_{x}\ .

See
6.10
.
Although this is formally obtained as a consequence of our results, in many ways one should think of this equivalence as the key technical ingredient needed in the proof of our main theorem.
Interestingly, the left hand side only depends on the equivalence class of
x
inside
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
, a statement that neatly encodes the
parallel transport
on the right hand side.

In the rest of the paper, we explore the consequences of the exodromy correspondence.
First, we obtain a couple of structural results for constructible hypersheaves:

Theorem 1.4

(See Corollaries
5.20
,
5.22
,
6.3
and
6.5
)
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category satisfying the assumptions of
5.17
.
Then:

(1)

Stability under limits and colimits:
the
\infty
-category
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
of
P
-hyperconstructible hypersheaves is presentable and closed under limits and colimits inside
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
;

(2)

Recognition criterion:
a hypersheaf
F\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
is
P
-hyperconstructible if and only if for every pair of open subsets
U\subseteq V
of
X
for which the induced morphism
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V,P)
is a categorical equivalence, the restriction map

F(V)\longrightarrow F(U)

is an equivalence in
\mathcal{E}
.

(3)

Tensor decomposition:
the canonical equivalence
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\simeq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}
restricts to an equivalence

\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\simeq\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{S})\otimes\mathcal{E}\ ;

(4)

Categorical Künneth formula:
given a second conically stratified space
(Y,Q)
with locally weakly contractible strata, there is a canonical equivalence

\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P\times Q}(X\times Y;\mathcal{S})\simeq\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{S})\otimes\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{Q}(Y;\mathcal{S})\ .

Observe that point (1) implies that the inclusion
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
admits both a left and a right adjoint.
Combined with
1.2
, it follows that for every morphism
f\colon(X,P)\to(Y,Q)
of conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata, the pullback functor

f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{Q}(Y;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P}(X;\mathcal{E})

admits a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
, a statement that is generically false for the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
(with the due exceptions, see
[
HPT
, §2.2]
).
Points (2), (3) and (4) are generalizations to the setting of hyperconstructible hypersheaves of the analogous results already obtained in
[
HPT
]
for locally hyperconstant hypersheaves.
As an immediate consequence of these results, we recover and generalize some of the results on Morita cohomology of J. V. Holstein
[
Ha
,
Hb
]
:

Corollary 1.5
.

Let
X
be a locally weakly contractible topological space and let
A
be a
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring spectrum.
Assume that
X
is connected and let
x\in X
be a point.
Then there exists a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{C}_{\ast}(\Omega_{x}(X);A)}\ ,

where
\mathrm{C}_{\ast}(\Omega_{x}(X);A)\coloneqq\Pi_{\infty}(\Omega_{x}(X))\otimes A
are the
A
-valued chains on the based loop space of
X
at
x
.

Last but not least, we combine the results of this paper together with the recent finiteness result for
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
of M. Volpe
[
V
, Corollary 2.13]
, to prove the following result:

Theorem 1.6

(cf. Theorems
7.8
&
7.32
)
.

Let
X
be a complex analytic space equipped with a Whitney stratification
(X,P)
.
Assume that for every
p\in P
, the stratum
X_{p}
is homotopically compact.
2
2

2

This means that
\Pi_{\infty}(X)
is a compact object in
\mathcal{S}
. This is a very mild condition that includes many cases of interest, see
7.3
.

Then there exists a higher Artin derived stack
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
parametrizing hyperconstructible hypersheaves on
(X,P)
.
Furthermore, for every perversity function
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
, there exists an open substack
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
of
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
parametrizing
\mathfrak{p}
-perverse hypersheaves on
X
.

In the main body of the paper we actually prove a finer result which does not require neither a complex analytic structure nor the stratification to be Whitney.
The analysis carried out suggests that Volpe’s finitness result can be strengthened and we provide a precise statement of the expected result in
7.10
.
Recall also that a similar result was obtained by Nitsure and Sabbah in
[
NS
]
.
The novelty in our case is the derived structure and the fact that
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
and
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
are sensitive to the higher homotopical information of
(X,P)
.
As an example of application of the derived structure, we obtain the following result:

Theorem 1.7
.

Let
X
be a Riemann surface and let
(X,P)
be a stratification on
X
made by a finite number of points.
Let
\mathfrak{p}
be the middle perversity function.
Then the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{b}}({}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X))
carries an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-monoidal structure
à la Hall
, whose underlying tensor product is given by pull-push along the correspondence

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{ext}}(X)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)\times{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Similarly, the Borel-Moore homology and the
G
-theory of
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
carry a canonical Hall multiplication.

Implicit in the above theorem is the fact that the map
p
is derived locally complete intersection and that
q
is representable by proper algebraic spaces (see
7.34
).
This result generalizes
[
PS
, Theorem 1.4]
, where the case of local systems was dealt with, and generalizes the cohomological Hall algebras of character varieties studied in
[
M
]
.
This raises new interesting questions in geometric representation theory, that are outlined in
7.36
and to which we will come back in a future work.
Finally, we expect
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
to carry a canonical shifted symplectic form, at least under some orientation condition on
(X,P)
that should conjecturally be related to Poincaré duality for the intersection homology of
(X,P)

[
AFTa
]
, and tightly related to the paper in preparation
[
APT
]
.

2.
Review

2.1.
Stratified spaces

The main reference for the definitions below is
[
HA
, Appendix A]
.

If
P
be a poset, we endow
P
with the topology whose open subsets are the closed upward subsets
Q\subset P
.
That is for every
a\in Q
and
b\in P
such that
b\geqslant a
, we have
b\in Q
.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
X
be a topological space.
Let
P
be a poset.
A
stratification of
X
by
P
is a continuous morphism
f\colon X\to P
.
For a subset
S\subset P
, we let
f|_{S}\colon X_{S}\to S
be the induced stratification.
For
a\in P
, the subset
X_{a}
is the stratum of
(X,P)
over
a
.

Remark 2.2
.

We abuse notations by denoting a stratification of
X
by
P
as
(X,P)
instead of
f\colon X\to P
and refer to
(X,P)
as a stratified space.
The collection of stratified spaces organize into a category in an obvious manner.

Example 2.3
.

Let
f\colon Y\to Q
be a stratified space.
Put
C(Y)\coloneqq\ast\sqcup\left(Y\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}\right)
.
The set
C(Y)
is endowed with the topology whose open subsets are the subsets
U\subset C(Y)
such that
U\cap\left(Y\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}\right)
is open and if
\ast\in U
, then

C_{\varepsilon}(Y):=\ast\sqcup\left(Y\times(0,\varepsilon)\right)\subset U

for some
\varepsilon>0
.
Let
Q^{\vartriangleleft}
be the poset obtained from
Q
by adding a smallest element
-\infty
.
We define a continuous map
g\colon C(Y)\to Q^{\vartriangleleft}
by sending
\ast
to
-\infty
and
(y,t)\in Y\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}
to
f(y)
.
We refer to
(C(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})
as the cone of
(Y,Q)
.

Following
[
HA
, A.6.2]
, we introduce the

Definition 2.4
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space.
We define
\mathrm{Exit}(X,P)
as the simplicial subset of
\Sing X
formed by the simplices
\sigma\colon|\Delta^{n}|\to X
such that there exists a chain
a_{1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant a_{n}
of elements of
P
such that for every
(t_{0},\dots,t_{i},0,\dots,0)\in|\Delta^{n}|
with
t_{i}>0
, we have

\sigma(t_{0},\dots,t_{i},0,\dots,0)\in X_{a_{i}}
.

The simplicial set
\mathrm{Exit}(X,P)
is the
exit-path simplicial set of
(X,P)
.

Notation 2.5
.

If
X
is trivially stratified
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X)
is the homotopy type
\Pi_{\infty}(X)
of
X
.
In virtue of this observation, we think of
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X)
as the
stratified homotopy type of
X

[
D
,
H
]
, and for this reason if
\mathrm{Exit}(X,P)
is an
\infty
-category we write
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
instead of
\mathrm{Exit}(X,P)
.

The following lemma follows immediately from the definition of the exit-paths.

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space.
Let
S\subset P
be a subset.
Assume that
\mathrm{Exit}(X,P)
and
\mathrm{Exit}(X_{S},S)
are
\infty
-categories.
Then, the natural inclusion
\mathrm{Exit}(X_{S},S)\to\mathrm{Exit}(X,P)
is fully-faithful.

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
(Y,Q)
be a stratified space such that
\mathrm{Exit}(C(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})
is an
\infty
-category.
Then,
\ast
is an initial object in
\mathrm{Exit}(C(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})
.

Proof.

Since
\ast
is the only point of its stratum,
\Map_{\mathrm{Exit}(C(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})}(*,*)
is contractible.
Let
(y,\varepsilon)\in Y\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
We are going to construct a deformation retract of

\Map_{\mathrm{Exit}(C(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})}(*,(y,\varepsilon))

on the simplicial subset spanned by the exit path
\gamma\colon[0,1]\to C(Y)
sending
0
to
\ast
and
t
to
(y,t\varepsilon)
.
To do this, we represent the above mapping space as
\Hom^{R}(\ast,(y,\varepsilon))
and construct an homotopy

H\colon\Hom^{R}(\ast,(y,\varepsilon))\times\Delta^{1}\longrightarrow\Hom^{R}(\ast,(y,\varepsilon))

between the map constant to
\gamma
and the identity of
\Hom^{R}(\ast,(y,\varepsilon))
.
At the cost of writing
\Hom^{R}(\ast,(y,\varepsilon))
as a colimit of its simplices
\sigma\colon\Delta^{n}\longrightarrow\Hom^{R}(\ast,(y,\varepsilon))
, it is enough to construct an homotopy
H
between the
n
-simplex constant to
\gamma
and
\sigma
.
That is, we have to construct

H\colon\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{1}\longrightarrow\Hom^{R}(\ast,(y,\varepsilon))

such that
H|_{\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\{0\}}}
is constantly equal to
\gamma
and
H|_{\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\{1\}}}=\sigma
.
Set
Z\coloneqq|\Delta^{n}|\times[0,1]
and
Z_{\lambda}\coloneqq|\Delta^{n}|\times\{\lambda\}
for every
\lambda\in[0,1]
.
By definition, the above construction amounts to the construction of a continuous map

H\colon Z\star\Delta^{0}\longrightarrow C(Y)

satisfying the following conditions :

(1)

H|_{Z_{0}\star\Delta^{0}}
is constantly equal to
\gamma
and
H|_{Z_{1}\star\Delta^{0}}=\sigma
.

(2)

H
sends the base of
Z\star\Delta^{0}
to
\ast
and the tip of
Z\star\Delta^{0}
to
(y,\varepsilon)
.

(3)

H
sends
Z\times]0,1]
in the stratum of
(y,\varepsilon)
.

For
(u,t)\in Z_{1}\times]0,1]
, put

\sigma(u,t)=(y(u,t),\varepsilon(u,t))\in Y\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}

By definition
\sigma(u,1)=(y,\varepsilon)
.
For
(u,\lambda,t)\in Z\star\Delta^{0}
, we define

H(u,\lambda,t)=\begin{cases}\big(y(u,t/\lambda),\lambda\varepsilon(u,t/\lambda)\big)&\text{if }0<t\leqslant\lambda\ ,\\
\gamma(t)&\text{if }\lambda\leqslant t\ ,\\
\ast&\text{if }t=0\ .\end{cases}

Observe that
H
satisfies conditions (1), (2), (3).
To conclude the proof of
2.7
, we have to show that
H
is continuous.
Away from
t=0
, the map
H
is continuous as the glueing of two continuous maps along
t=\lambda
.
Let
(u_{0},\lambda_{0},0)\in Z\star\Delta^{0}
.
We want to check that
H
is continuous at
(u_{0},\lambda_{0},0)
.
If
\lambda_{0}>0
, we have to check that
H
is continuous on the open set
\lambda>t
, which is true since
\sigma
is continuous.
The continuity of
H
at
(u_{0},0,0)
follows from the definition of the topology of
C(Y)
and the observation that
\varepsilon
is bounded.
∎

Definition 2.8
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space.
We say that
(X,P)
is
conically stratified
if for every point
x\in X
lying over
a\in P
, there exists a topological space
Z
, a stratified space
(Y,P_{>a})
and a morphism of stratified spaces
(Z\times C(Y),P_{\geqslant a})\to(X,P)
inducing an homeomorphism between
Z\times C(Y)
and an open neighbourhood of
x
in
X
.

Remark 2.9
.

The open sub-stratified space
(Z\times C(Y),P_{\geqslant a})
of
(X,P)
in
2.8
is a conical chart of
(X,P)
at
x
.
By definition of the topology of
C(Y)
, the set of conical charts of
(X,P)
at
x
form a fundamental system of open neighbourhoods of
x
in
X
.

Conically stratified spaces enjoy the following stability property:

Lemma 2.10
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
S\subset P
be a subset.
Then
(X_{S},S)
is a conically stratified space.

Proof.

Let
x\in X_{S}
and let
s\in S
be the stratum of
x
.
We have to show that
(X_{S},S)
is conical at
x
.
The question is local on
X_{S}
.
At the cost of replacing
X
by the open set
X_{\geqslant s}
, we can thus suppose that
s
is the minimum of
P
.
Since
X
is conical at
x
, we can suppose further that
(X,P)
is of the form
(Z\times C(Y),P)
where
Z
is a topological space and where
(Y,P_{>s})
is a stratified space.
Then
X_{S}=Z\times C(Y_{S_{>s}})
and
2.10
is proved.
∎

The following theorem due to Lurie
[
HA
, A.6.4]
provides a wide range of stratified spaces whose exit-paths form an
\infty
-category:

Theorem 2.11
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Then
\mathrm{Exit}(X,P)
is an
\infty
-category.

Remark 2.12
.

In view of
2.11
and
2.5
, the
\infty
-category of exit-paths of a conically stratified space
(X,P)
will be denoted as
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
.

Definition 2.13
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
We say that
(X,P)
is
locally weakly contractible
if every point
x\in X
admits a fundamental system of open neighbourhoods
U
such that
x
is an initial object of
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)
.

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
f\colon X\to X^{\prime}
be a weak homotopy equivalence and let
(Y,Q)
be a stratified space.
Then the induced map

\mathrm{Exit}(X\times Y,Q)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Exit}(X^{\prime}\times Y,Q)

is a categorical equivalence of simplicial sets.

Proof.

Unraveling the definitions, we find canonical
isomorphisms
of simplicial sets

\mathrm{Exit}(X\times Y,Q)\simeq\Sing(X)\times\mathrm{Exit}(Y,Q)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{Exit}(X^{\prime}\times Y,Q)\simeq\Sing(X^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Exit}(Y,Q)\ .

Since the map
f
induces a categorical equivalence
\Sing(X)\to\Sing(X^{\prime})
, the conclusion now follows from
[
HTT
, Corollary 2.2.5.4]
.
∎

Proposition 2.15
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
The following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

(X,P)
is locally weakly contractible.

(2)

The strata of
(X,P)
are locally weakly contractible.

Remark 2.16
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space whose strata are CW-complexes.
Then
2.15
states that
(X,P)
is locally weakly contractible if and only if its strata are locally contractible.

Proof of
2.15
.

2.6
shows that
(1)
implies
(2)
.
Assume now that
(2)
holds.
Let
x\in X
be a point and let
a
be its image in
P
.
Since
X
is conical, we can suppose that
X=Z\times C(Y)
where
(Y,Q)
is a stratified space with
a
the minimum of
P=Q^{\vartriangleleft}
.
Let
\mathcal{W}_{x}
be a fundamental system of weakly contractible open neighborhoods of
x
in
Z
.
Then
\{W\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y)\}_{W\in\mathcal{W}_{x},\varepsilon>0}
is a fundamental system of open neighborhoods of
x
inside
X
.
For
W\in\mathcal{W}_{x}
and
\varepsilon>0
, we have an isomorphism of simplicial sets

\mathrm{Exit}(W\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y),P)\simeq\Sing(W)\times\mathrm{Exit}(C_{\varepsilon}(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})

On the other hand, if
K
and
L
are non empty simplicial sets such that
K\times L
is an
\infty
-category, so are
K
and
L
.
Thus,
2.11
ensures that
\mathrm{Exit}(C_{\varepsilon}(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})
is an
\infty
-category.
Since
W
is weakly contractible, we obtain an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{Exit}(W\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y),P)\simeq\mathrm{Exit}(C_{\varepsilon}(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})

sending
x
to
\ast\in C(Y)
.
We conclude using
2.7
.
∎

2.2.
Local excellence at strata

Definition 2.17
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
S\subset P
be a subposet.
We say that an open neighbourhood
U
of
X_{S}
inside
X
is
excellent at
S
if the functor

\mathrm{Exit}(X_{S},S)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Exit}(U,P)

is a final.

Definition 2.18
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
S\subset P
be a subposet.
We say that
(X,P)
is
excellent at
S
if the collection of excellent at
S
open neighbourhoods of
X_{S}
inside
X
forms a fundamental system of neighbourhoods of
X_{S}
inside
X
.

Definition 2.19
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
S\subset P
be a subposet.
We say that
(X,P)
is
locally excellent at
S
if every point
x\in X_{S}
admits a fundamental system of open neighbourhoods
U
such that the stratified space
(U,P)
is excellent at
S
.

Proposition 2.20
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Then
(X,P)
is locally excellent at every stratum.

Proof.

Let
a\in P
and let
x\in X
be a point lying over
a
.
Since
(X,P)
is conically stratified, the point
x
admits a fundamental system of open neighbourhoods of the form
Z\times C(Y)
where
(Y,Q)
is a stratified space, where
Z
is an open set of
X_{a}
containing
x
and where
a
is the minimum of
P_{\geqslant a}=Q^{\vartriangleleft}
.
To prove
2.20
, it is thus enough to prove that the conically stratified space
(Z\times C(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})
is excellent at
a
.
In that case,
\{Z\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y)\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is a fundamental system of open neighborhoods of
Z
.
On the other hand, there is an isomorphism of simplicial sets

\mathrm{Exit}(Z\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})\simeq\Sing(Z)\times\mathrm{Exit}(C_{\varepsilon}(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})

Hence, the functor

\mathrm{Exit}(Z\times\ast,\{a\})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Exit}(Z\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})

reads as a product

(2.21)

\Sing(Z)\times\ast\longrightarrow\Sing(Z)\times\mathrm{Exit}(C_{\varepsilon}(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})

From
2.7
, the functor
\ast\to\mathrm{Exit}(C_{\varepsilon}(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})
is final.
Thus,
Eq.

2.21
is final as a consequence of
[
HTT
, 4.1.1.13]
.
∎

2.3.
Hypersheaves, hyperconstancy and hyperconstructibility

Definition 2.22
.

Let
X
be a topological space.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
We denote by
\mathrm{Sh}(X;\mathcal{E})
the full subcategory of
\Fun(\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})
spanned by
\mathcal{E}
-valued presheaves satisfying descent.

Remark 2.23
.

If
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{S}
, we simply denote
\mathrm{Sh}(X;\mathcal{E})
by
\mathrm{Sh}(X)
.
In general, we have
\mathrm{Sh}(X;\mathcal{E})\simeq\mathrm{Sh}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}
, where the tensor product denotes the tensor product of presentable
\infty
-categories introduced in
[
HA
, §4.8]
.

Definition 2.24
.

The category of
hypersheaves on
X
is the
subcategory
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}(X)
spanned by objects which are local with respect to
\infty
-connective morphisms in
\mathrm{Sh}(X)
.
For a presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
, we put

\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\coloneqq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}\simeq\FunR(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})

Remark 2.25
.

The canonical inclusion
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\subset\mathrm{Sh}(X)
admits a left adjoint
(-)^{\mathrm{hyp}}:\mathrm{Sh}(X)\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
referred to as the hypersheafification functor.
Tensoring with
\mathcal{E}
thus induces a functor

(-)^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}(X;\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})

admitting a fully-faithful right adjoint.
Hence, we can think of objects of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
as
\mathcal{E}
-valued preseheaves satisfying hyperdescent.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of topological spaces.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Let

f^{\ast}\colon\mathrm{Sh}(Y;\mathcal{E})\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Sh}(X;\mathcal{E})\colon f_{\ast}

be the canonical adjunction.
The functor
f_{*}
preserve hypersheaves and thus restricts to a functor

f_{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})

Furthermore, the functor
f_{*}
admits

f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\coloneqq(-)^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f^{*}:\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E}).

as left adjoint.

In the next definition,
\Gamma_{X}:X\to\ast
denotes the tautological morphism.

Definition 2.26
.

Fix
F\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
We say that
F
is
hyperconstant
if
F
lies in the essential image of
\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

We say that
F
is
locally hyperconstant
if there exists a cover of
X
by open subsets
i\colon U\to X
such that
i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
is hyperconstant.
We denote by
\mathrm{LC}(X;\mathcal{E})
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
spanned by locally hyperconstant hypersheaves.

Following
[
L
]
, we now introduce the main player of this paper:

Definition 2.27
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
An hypersheaf
F\colon\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{E}
with value in
\mathcal{E}
is
hyperconstructible
if for every
p\in P
, the hypersheaf
j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
is locally hyperconstant on
X_{p}
, where
j_{p}\colon X_{p}\to X
denotes the canonical inclusion.
We denote by
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
the full-subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
spanned by hyperconstructible hypersheaves on
(X,P)
.

2.4.
Change of coefficients and hyperconstructibility

Let
X
be a topological space.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
be a cocontinuous functor between presentable
\infty
-categories with right adjoint
g\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
.
Then, there is an adjunction

(2.28)

f\circ-\colon\Fun(\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})\leftrightarrows\Fun(\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})\colon g\circ-

whose left adjoint induces a cocontinuous functor

f^{\mathrm{hyp}}\coloneqq(-)^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f\circ-\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})\ .

Furthermore, for every continuous morphism of topological spaces
i:Y\to X
, the following diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{D})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}

commutes.
In particular, if
(X,P)
is a stratified space,
f^{\mathrm{hyp}}
restricts to a functor

f^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})\ .

Remark 2.29
.

Note that
f^{\mathrm{hyp}}
may not have a right adjoint in general.
We will show that when Exodromy holds, a right adjoint exists and admits an explicit description.
See
6.16
.

Lemma 2.30
.

Let
X
be a locally weakly contractible topological space.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
be a cocontinuous functor between presentable
\infty
-categories with right adjoint
g:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
.
Then, the commutative diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\circ-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{X,\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{X,\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

is vertically left adjointable. That is, the Beck-Chevalley transformation

\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ g\longrightarrow g\circ\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\

is an equivalence.
In particular,
g\circ-
restricts to a well-defined functor

g\circ-\colon\mathrm{LC}(X,\mathcal{D})\longrightarrow\mathrm{LC}(X,\mathcal{E})\

Proof.

Let
d\in\mathcal{D}
be an object.
We have to show that

\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}g(d)\longrightarrow g\circ\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(d)

is an equivalence of hypersheaves on
X
.
It is enough to check that the above morphism is an equivalence above any weakly contractible open subset
U
of
X
.
From
[
HPT
, Theorem 2.13]
, we have

(\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ g(d))(U):=\big(\Gamma_{X}^{-1}(g(d))\big)^{\mathrm{hyp}}(U)\simeq(\Gamma_{X}^{-1}\circ g(d))(U)\simeq g(d)

From
[
HPT
, Theorem 2.13]
again, we have

(g\circ\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(d))(U)=g\big((\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(d))(U)\big)\simeq g(d)

and
2.30
follows.
∎

3.
The categorical framework

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-category and let
\mathsf{A}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
be a functor with values in small
\infty
-categories.
Throughout this section we either assume
\mathcal{X}
to be a small
\infty
-category with a terminal object
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}
or to be presentable.
In the latter case, we assume
\mathsf{A}
to be an accessible functor.
We let

\pi_{\mathsf{A}}\colon\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}

be the associated
cartesian
fibration.
Writing
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}
for the terminal object of
\mathcal{X}
,
[
HTT
, Corollary 3.3.4.3]
provides a canonical
localization
functor

\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}\colon\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}).

Example 3.1
.

In this paper we are mainly interested in the following situations:

(1)

Let
X
be a topological space.
Take
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{Open}(X)
and
\mathsf{A}\coloneqq\Pi_{\infty}
the functor
\mathsf{Open}(X)\to\mathcal{S}
sending an open set
U
to its homotopy type
\Pi_{\infty}(U)
.
We will also take
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{PSh}(X)
and
A\colon\mathrm{PSh}(X)\to\mathcal{S}
the colimit-preserving functor obtained as left Kan extension of
\Pi_{\infty}
along the Yoneda embedding.
From
[
HA
, A.3.10]
,
\mathsf{A}
carries
\infty
-connective morphisms in
\mathrm{PSh}(X)
to equivalences in
\mathcal{S}
.
If we finally put
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
, the functor
\mathsf{A}
thus factors as a colimit-preserving functor issued from
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
.

(2)

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified topological space.
Take
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{Open}(X)
and
\mathsf{A}\coloneqq\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}
the functor
\mathsf{Open}(X)\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
sending an open set
U
to
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P|_{U})
.
We will also take
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{PSh}(X)
and
\mathsf{A}\colon\mathrm{PSh}(X)\to\mathcal{S}
the colimit-preserving functor obtained as left Kan extension of
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}
along the Yoneda embedding.
Similarly as in situation
(1)
,
\mathsf{A}
carries
\infty
-connective morphisms in
\mathrm{PSh}(X)
to equivalences in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
If we finally put
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
, the functor
\mathsf{A}
thus factors as a colimit-preserving functor issued from
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
. We will abuse notations by using the notation
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}
to also refer to the functors
\mathsf{A}
and its factorization through
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
.

Remark 3.2
.

Situation (1) is a special case of situation (2) in the same way monodromy is a special case of exodromy.
Still, the proof of the latter is a reduction to the trivial stratification case.
It will therefore be useful to analyze the situation (1) on its own.

3.1.
Size issues

The main point of this paper is to show that the exodromy adjunction is realized by push-pull along the correspondence
\pi_{\mathsf{A}}\times\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})
.
When
\mathcal{X}
is a presentable
\infty
-category, there may be size-theoretical issues in defining the right Kan extension along
\pi_{\mathsf{A}}
and the left Kan extension along
\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}
.
The following lemmas show this is not an actual problem:

Lemma 3.3
.

For every
x\in\mathcal{X}
, the canonical functor

\mathsf{A}(x)\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}})_{x/}

is limit-final.
In particular, for every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
, the pullback functor

\pi_{\mathsf{A}}^{\ast}\colon\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{E})

admits a right adjoint, given by right Kan extension along
\pi_{\mathsf{A}}
.

Proof.

The second half of the statement is a direct consequence of the first half and the fact that
\mathsf{A}(x)
is a small
\infty
-category, which implies that the right Kan extension along
p
is indeed well-defined.
To prove the first half, we use Quillen’s theorem A
[
HTT
, Theorem 4.1.3.1]
.
Write
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}})_{x/}
.
We can represent an object
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{D}
as a pair
\mathbf{a}=(a,f)
where
a
belongs to
\mathcal{A}
and
f\colon x\to\pi_{\mathsf{A}}(a)
is a morphism in
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
We have to prove that for every object
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{D}
, the
\infty
-category

\mathsf{A}(x)\times_{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathbf{a}}

is weakly contractible.
By definition, an object of
\mathsf{A}(x)\times_{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathbf{a}}
is a lift of
f
with target
a
.
In particular,
\mathsf{A}(x)\times_{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{D}_{/\mathbf{a}}
identifies with
\mathcal{A}_{/a}\times_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}_{/\pi_{\mathsf{A}}(a)}}\{f\}
.
From
[
HTT
, 2.4.1.9]
, the latter category admits a cartesian lift of
f
as final object.
It is thus weakly contractible.
∎

It is slightly more technical to deal with the left Kan extension along
\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}
.
It is useful to introduce the following definition:

Definition 3.4
.

Let
\kappa
be a regular cardinal.
We say that a functor
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is
\kappa
-cofiltered if for every essentially
\kappa
-small
\infty
-category
I
, the functor
f
has the right lifting property against the inclusion
I\hookrightarrow I^{\lhd}
.
When
\kappa=\omega
, we simply say that
f
is cofiltered instead of
\omega
-cofiltered.

The proof of the following lemma is straightforward and it is left to the reader:

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
\kappa
be a regular cardinal.
Then:

(1)

the collection of
\kappa
-filtered functors is closed under composition and pullback in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
;

(2)

the functor
\Gamma_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathcal{C}\to*
is
\kappa
-filtered if and only if
\mathcal{C}
is
\kappa
-filtered.

Lemma 3.6
.

Choose a regular cardinal
\kappa\gg 0
such that
\mathcal{X}
is
\kappa
-presentable and
\mathsf{A}
is
\kappa
-accessible.
Let

\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}\coloneqq(\mathcal{X}^{\kappa})^{\mathrm{op}}\times_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{A}.

Then for every
\alpha\in\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})
the canonical functor

\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}\times_{\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})}\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})_{/\alpha}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})}\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})_{/\alpha}

is colimit-final.
In particular, for every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
, the pullback functor

\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}^{\ast}\colon\Fun(\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}),\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{E})

admits a left adjoint, given by left Kan extension along
\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}
.

Proof.

The second half of the statement is a direct consequence of the first half and the fact that
\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}\times_{\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})}\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})_{/\alpha}
is a small
\infty
-category, which implies that the left Kan extension along
\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}
is indeed well-defined.
To prove the first half, we use Quillen’s theorem A.
Write for simplicity

\mathcal{A}_{/\alpha}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})}\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})_{/\alpha}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}_{/\alpha}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}\times_{\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})}\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})_{/\alpha}.

Fix an object
\mathbf{\beta}\in\mathcal{A}_{/\alpha}
, which we represent as a pair
\mathbf{\beta}=(\beta,f)
, where
\beta\in\mathcal{A}
and
f\colon\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}(\beta)\to\alpha
is a morphism in
\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})
.
Write
\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{\beta}/\!\!/\alpha}\coloneqq(\mathcal{A}_{/\alpha})_{\mathbf{\beta}/}
.
Then, we have to prove that
\infty
-category

\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}_{\mathbf{\beta}/\!\!/\alpha}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}_{/\alpha}\times_{\mathcal{A}_{/\alpha}}\mathcal{A}_{\mathbf{\beta}/\!\!/\alpha}

is weakly contractible.
We claim that it is cofiltered.
To see this, start by writing
x\coloneqq p(\beta)
, so that
\beta
can be seen as an element of
\mathsf{A}(x)
, and
\mathbf{\beta}
as an element of
\mathsf{A}(x)_{/\alpha}\coloneqq\mathsf{A}(x)\times_{\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})}\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})_{/\alpha}
.
We can thus set
\mathsf{A}(x)_{\mathbf{\beta}/\!\!/\alpha}\coloneqq(\mathsf{A}(x)_{/\alpha})_{\mathbf{\beta}/}
.
Observe now that since
\pi_{\mathsf{A}}
is a cartesian fibration, there is an induced commutative diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}_{\mathbf{\beta}/\!\!/\alpha}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}_{\beta/}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}(x)_{\mathbf{\beta}/\!\!/\alpha}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}(x)_{\beta/}\ .}

Inspection immediately reveals that it is a pullback square.
Moreover,
\mathsf{A}(x)_{\mathbf{\beta}/\!\!/\alpha}
has an initial object, and it is in particular cofiltered.
Thus,
3.5
reduces us to check that the map
\mathcal{A}^{(\kappa)}_{\beta/}\to\mathsf{A}(x)_{\beta/}
is cofiltered.
Unraveling the definitions, we see that it is enough to check that for every finite category
I
the canonical map

\colim_{y\in\mathcal{X}^{\kappa}_{/x}}\Fun(I,\mathsf{A}(y)_{\beta/})\longrightarrow\Fun(I,\mathsf{A}(x)_{\beta/})

is an equivalence.
This, however, is guaranteed from the assumption that
\mathcal{X}
is
\kappa
-filtered and that the functor
\mathsf{A}
is
\kappa
-accessible.
∎

Fix a presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
.

3.3
provides a functor

\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\pi_{\mathsf{A},\ast}\circ\,\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}^{\ast}\colon\Fun(\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}),\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E}).

From
3.3
, this functor is concretely described by the formula

(3.7)

\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}(F)(x)\simeq\lim_{\mathsf{A}(x)}F|_{\mathsf{A}(x)}\ ,

where the restriction is performed along the map
\mathsf{A}(x)\to\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})
induced by the canonical morphism
x\to\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}
.
On the other hand,
3.6
shows the existence of a second functor

\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\lambda_{A,!}\circ\pi^{\ast}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}),\mathcal{E}).

By construction,
\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
is left adjoint to
\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
.

3.2.
Change of coefficients

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
For every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
, there is a canonical equivalence

\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E})\otimes\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\simeq\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{\prime})\ .

This gives an evident functoriality in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
with respect to morphisms in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
.
To see that the exodromy adjunction is compatible with this functoriality, consider first the following lemma:

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-category with a terminal object
1_{\mathcal{X}}
.
Let
\mathsf{A}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
be a functor.
For every presentable
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, there is a canonical equivalence

\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\simeq\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\ .

Proof.

Since
\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
commutes with colimits, it is enough to observe that for every
F\in\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})
and
E^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, one has

\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}(F\otimes E^{\prime})\coloneqq\lambda_{\mathsf{A},!}(\pi_{\mathsf{A}}^{\ast}(F\otimes E^{\prime}))\simeq\lambda_{\mathsf{A},!}(\pi_{\mathsf{A}}^{\ast}(F)\otimes E^{\prime})\simeq\lambda_{\mathsf{A},!}(\pi_{\mathsf{A}}^{\ast}(F))\otimes E^{\prime}\ ,

where the last equivalence follows from the fact that the external tensor product
\otimes\colon\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
commutes with the colimits computing the left Kan extension in the first variable.
∎

Corollary 3.9
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-category with a terminal object
1_{\mathcal{X}}
.
Let
\mathsf{A}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
be a functor.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
be a morphism in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
.
Then the diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}),\mathcal{E}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}

is canonically commutative.

Proof.

This follows from
3.8
and the observation that under the identification
\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E})\simeq\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S})\otimes\mathcal{E}
for
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
, the functor
f\circ-
corresponds to
\mathrm{id}_{\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S})}\otimes f
.
∎

3.3.
The sheaf condition

Assume now that
\mathcal{X}
is presentable and that the functor
\mathsf{A}
commutes with colimits.
Then the adjunction
\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
can be refined thanks to the following observation:

Lemma 3.10
.

Assume that
\mathsf{A}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
commutes with colimits.
Then
\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
factors through the full subcategory
\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{E}\simeq\FunR(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})
of
\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

Fix a functor
F\colon\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})\to\mathcal{E}
and let
I\to\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
be a diagram, noted
i\mapsto x_{i}
, and let
x
denote the limit of this diagram.
By assumption, the canonical map

\colim_{i\in I^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathsf{A}(x_{i})\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}(x)

is an equivalence, the colimit being computed in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
It follows that the canonical map

\Fun(\mathsf{A}(x),\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\lim_{i\in I}\Fun(\mathsf{A}(x_{i}),\mathcal{E})

is an equivalence as well.
Applying
[
PY
, §8.2]
, we deduce a canonical equivalence

\lim_{\mathsf{A}(x)}F|_{\mathsf{A}(x)}\simeq\lim_{i\in I}\lim_{\mathsf{A}(x_{i})}F|_{\mathsf{A}(x_{i})}\ .

Thus,
\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}(F)
commutes with limits in
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

Assume that
\mathsf{A}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
commutes with colimits.
Then for any presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
, the functors
\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
and
\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
induce an adjunction

\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{E}\leftrightarrows\Fun(\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}),\mathcal{E})\colon\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\ .

Example 3.12
.

Let us place ourselves again in the context of
3.1
.
Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
To lighten the notation, we write

\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\Phi_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X),\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}}^{\mathcal{E}}\quad\text{and}\quad\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\Psi_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X),\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}}^{\mathcal{E}}\ .

When the stratification is trivial we further simplify these notations by removing the subscript
P
, so that in this case
\Pi_{\infty}=\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}
.
When
X
and
\mathcal{E}
are clear from the context we also remove the corresponding decoration.
Given
F\in\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})
,
3.10
shows that the functor
\Psi(F)
belongs to
\FunR(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)^{\mathrm{op}};\mathcal{E})\simeq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
The previous discussion shows that for an open subset
U
of
X
,
\Psi(F)(U)
is canonically given by the formula

(3.13)

\Psi(F)(U)\simeq\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P|_{U})}F|_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P|_{U})}.

Finally, we have obvious variants

\displaystyle\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh},\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\Phi_{\mathrm{PSh}(X),\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}}^{\mathcal{E}}\ ,\qquad\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh},\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\Psi_{\mathrm{PSh}(X),\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}}^{\mathcal{E}}\ ,

\displaystyle\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\Phi_{\mathrm{Open}(X),\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}}^{\mathcal{E}}\ ,\qquad\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\Psi_{\mathrm{Open}(X),\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}}^{\mathcal{E}}\ ,

obtained replacing
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
by
\mathrm{PSh}(X)
and
\mathrm{Open}(X)
respectively.

3.4.
Functoriality

Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a functor.
We assume that
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are either small with final objects, or presentable (importantly, we allow
\mathcal{X}
to be of the first kind and
\mathcal{Y}
to be of the second).
Furthermore, we assume that
f(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})\simeq\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, and if both
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are presentable then we additionally assume that
f
is a left adjoint.
Let

\mathsf{B}\colon\mathcal{Y}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{A}\colon\mathcal{X}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}

be two functors and let

\gamma\colon\mathsf{B}\circ f\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}

be a natural transformation.
We can summarize this information in the following diagram:

(3.14)

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{f}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathsf{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
\mathcal{B}_{f}
and the maps
\pi_{f}
and
q
are defined by declaring that the bottom left square is a pullback,
p
is induced by the natural transformation
\gamma
and
\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}\coloneqq\gamma_{\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}}
is the value of
\gamma
on the final object of
\mathcal{X}
.

Lemma 3.15
.

For every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
, the diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{B}_{f},\mathcal{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{B}}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\ast}}

is vertically right adjointable.

Proof.

The existence of the right adjoints to
\pi_{\mathsf{A}}^{\ast}
and
\pi_{\mathsf{B}}^{\ast}
follows from
3.3
.
In order to check that the Beck-Chevalley transformation

f^{\ast}\circ\pi_{\mathsf{B},\ast}\longrightarrow\pi_{f,\ast}\circ q^{\ast}

is an equivalence, it is enough to fix
F\in\Fun(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{E})
and
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Then
3.3
identifies
f^{\ast}(\pi_{\mathsf{B},\ast}(F))(x)
and
\pi_{f,\ast}(q^{\ast}(F))(x)
with

\lim_{\mathsf{B}(f(x))}F|_{\mathsf{B}(f(x))}.

The conclusion follows.
∎

Notation 3.16
.

Assume that
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are presentable and that
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
commutes with colimits.
For any presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
set
f_{\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq f\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{E}
.
We let
g_{\mathcal{E}}
denote the right adjoint to
f_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Corollary 3.17
.

Fix a presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
.

(1)

Assume that
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are small.
Then the diagrams

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{B}\circ f}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}}

and

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\mathcal{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{B}\circ f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}}

are canonically commutative.

(2)

Assume that
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are presentable and that
\mathsf{B}
and
f
commute with colimits.
Then the diagrams

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{B}\circ f}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{\mathcal{E}}}

and

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\mathcal{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{B}\circ f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}}

are canonically commutative.

Proof.

It is enough to prove the commutativity of the left triangle of
(1)
.
Breaking
\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}
into its components, we have to show that the squares of the following diagram commute:

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{B}_{f},\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathsf{B}}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{B},\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f,\ast}}

For the left square, this just follows obviously from the definition of the functors.
For the right square, this is a consequence of
3.15
.
∎

Example 3.18
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified topological space.

(1)

Take
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{Open}(X)
,
\mathcal{Y}\coloneqq\mathrm{PSh}(X)
and take
f
to be the Yoneda embedding.
We take
\mathsf{A}\coloneqq\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}
, the exit paths
\infty
-functor and we take
\mathsf{B}
to be the left Kan extension of
\mathsf{A}
along
f
.
Then

f^{\ast}\colon\FunR(\mathrm{PSh}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})

is an equivalence as a consequence of
[
HTT
, Theorem 5.1.5.6]
.
Thus
3.10

(1)
and
3.17
yield a natural equivalence

\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh},\mathcal{E}}\ .

3.17
also identifies
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh},\mathcal{E}}
with the left Kan extension of
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathcal{E}}
along the Yoneda embedding.

(2)

Take
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{PSh}(X)
,
\mathcal{Y}\coloneqq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
and take
f\coloneqq(-)^{\mathrm{hyp}}
to be the hypersheafification functor.
In this case,
3.17
yields natural equivalences

\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh},\mathcal{E}}\simeq\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh},\mathcal{E}}\simeq\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\circ(-)^{\mathrm{hyp}}\ .

In particular, we see that for every
F\in\mathrm{PSh}(X;\mathcal{E})
, the functor
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathcal{E}}
takes the natural transformation
F\to F^{\mathrm{hyp}}
to an equivalence.

Construction 3.19
.

Associated to the top right square in the diagram (
3.14
) there is, for every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
, a Beck-Chevalley transformation

\mathsf{BC}\colon\lambda_{f,!}\circ p^{\ast}\longrightarrow\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\lambda_{\mathsf{A},!}\ ,

which induces the following transformation

\phi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}\colon\Phi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{B}\circ f}^{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\lambda_{f,!}\circ\pi_{f}^{\ast}\simeq\lambda_{f,!}p^{\ast}\pi_{\mathsf{A}}^{\ast}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{BC}}\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\lambda_{A,!}\circ\pi_{A}^{\ast}\simeq\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\ .

In turn, associated to
\phi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}
there is a natural exchange transformation

\psi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}\colon f_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{\eta_{\mathcal{Y}}}\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\Phi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}f_{\mathcal{E}}\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{\phi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}}\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\mathcal{X}}}\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\circ\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\ ,

where
\eta_{\mathcal{Y}}
denotes the unit of the adjunction
\Phi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}
and
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{X}}
denotes the counit of the adjunction
\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
.
In the rest of the paper we will often write
\phi_{f}
and
\psi_{f}
instead of
\phi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}
and
\psi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}
, respectively.

Remark 3.20
.

(1)

The triangular identities imply that the exchange transformation associated to
\psi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}
is once again
\phi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}
.
In particular, if one knows that both
\Phi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}
and
\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
are equivalences, then
\phi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}
is an equivalence if and only if
\psi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}
is one.
Furthermore, triangular identities also imply that the square

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\Phi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}f}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{Y}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\eta_{\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{Y}}\phi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{f}\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}}

is canonically commutative.

(2)

Suppose that the natural transformation
\psi_{f}
is an equivalence, so that it renders the square

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}}

commutative.
Then this square is horizontally right adjointable
if and only if
the natural transformation
\phi_{f}
is an equivalence as well.
Thus, if both
\phi_{f}
and
\psi_{f}
are equivalences, it follows formally that
f_{\mathcal{E}}
takes the unit of the adjunction
\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
to the unit of the adjunction
\Phi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}
.
Similarly,
\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}
takes the counit of the adjunction
\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}
to the counit of the adjunction
\Phi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}
.

Corollary 3.21
.

Assume that
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are presentable, that
\mathsf{B}
commutes with colimits, that
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
has a left adjoint
h\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
and that the induced transformation

\mathsf{B}\xrightarrow{\eta}\mathsf{B}\circ f\circ h\xrightarrow{\gamma}A\circ h

is an equivalence, where
\eta
is the unit of the adjunction
h\dashv f
.
Then the natural transformation

\psi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}\colon f_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow\Psi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}\circ\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Indeed, since
f
has a left adjoint
h
, the same goes for
f_{\mathcal{E}}
and moreover under the equivalences
\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{E}\simeq\FunR(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})
and
\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{E}\simeq\FunL(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{E})
, the functor
f_{\mathcal{E}}
corresponds to
h^{\ast}
.
Unwinding the definitions and using
3.3
, we see that for every
F\in\Fun(\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}),\mathcal{E})
and every
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}
, the map
\psi_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},f,\gamma}(F)
at
y
is given by

\lim_{\mathsf{A}(h(y))}F|_{\mathsf{A}(h(y))}\longrightarrow\lim_{\mathsf{B}(y)}(F\circ u)|_{\mathsf{B}(y)}\ .

Thus, the conclusion follows from the fact that the functor
\mathsf{B}(y)\to\mathsf{A}(h(y))
is an equivalence.
∎

Example 3.22
.

(1)

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces.
Take
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
and
\mathcal{Y}\coloneqq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y)
and
f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y)
the induced geometric morphism.
We let
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{B}
be the exit paths functors

\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty},

respectively.
The functoriality of the exit path construction yields a natural transformation
\gamma\colon\mathsf{B}\circ f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\to\mathsf{A}
.
In this setup, we denote by

\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Phi_{Y}^{\mathcal{E}}\circ f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\circ\Phi_{X}^{\mathcal{E}}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Psi_{X}^{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow\Psi_{Y}^{\mathcal{E}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}

the natural transformations of
3.19
.
Taking
\mathcal{X}=\mathrm{PSh}(X)
and
\mathcal{Y}=\mathrm{PSh}(Y)
or
\mathcal{X}=\mathrm{Open}(X)
and
\mathcal{Y}=\mathrm{Open}(Y)
we obtain obvious variants of this construction, that we denote respectively by
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}
,
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}
,
\phi_{f}
and
\psi_{f}
.

(2)

In the setting of the previous point, assume furthermore that the underlying morphism of topological spaces
f\colon Y\to X
is an open immersion.
Then
f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y)
admits a fully faithful
left
adjoint, denoted
f_{!}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
.
Furthermore, if
U
is an open subset of
Y
, seen as an object in
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y)
, then
f_{!}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(U)\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
represents again
U
, seen as an open in
X
.
It follows that the assumptions of
3.21
are satisfied and therefore that
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence in this case.

3.5.
A glimpse of higher-functoriality

The natural transformations
\phi_{f}
and
\psi_{f}
of
3.19
enjoy themselves a form of higher functoriality.
We fix two composable morphisms
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
and
h\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{Z}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
We further fix three functors

\mathsf{A}\colon\mathcal{X}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty},\qquad\mathsf{B}\colon\mathcal{Y}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty},\qquad\mathsf{C}\colon\mathcal{Z}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}\ ,

together with natural transformations

\gamma\colon\mathsf{B}\circ f\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\delta\colon\mathsf{C}\circ h\longrightarrow\mathsf{B}\ .

As in the previous section, we assume that
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{Y}
,
\mathcal{Z}
are either small with a final objects or presentable.
As for
f
and
h
, we assume that they preserve final objects and, if both their source and target are presentable, that they commute with colimits.

Fix a presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
.
In this situation,
3.19
provides three natural transformations

\phi_{h}\colon\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathsf{C}}\circ h_{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow\delta_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}\ ,\qquad\phi_{f}\colon\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}

as well as

\phi_{h\!f}\colon\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathsf{C}}\circ h_{\mathcal{E}}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow\delta_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}\ .

We have:

Proposition 3.23
.

The triangle

(3.24)

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathsf{C}}\circ h_{\mathcal{E}}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{h}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{h\!f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{f}}

is canonically commutative.

Proof.

Consider the following diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{C}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Z}})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{f}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{h}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{h\!f}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathsf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{\mathbf{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathsf{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathsf{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathsf{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{h\!f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{h\!f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Using
3.17
twice, we find canonical equivalences

\alpha\colon\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathsf{C}}\circ h_{\mathcal{E}}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\lambda_{h\!f,!}\circ\pi_{h\!f}^{\ast},\qquad\beta\colon\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\lambda_{f,!}\circ\pi_{f}^{\ast}\ .

Write
\sigma_{1}
for the square whose edges are
(\lambda_{f},\delta_{\mathbf{1}},\lambda_{h\!f},s)
and
\sigma_{2}
for the square with edges
(\lambda_{\mathsf{A}},\gamma_{\mathbf{1}},\lambda_{f},u)
.
We also let
\sigma_{12}
denote the rectangle obtained juxtaposing
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
.
Write

\mathsf{BC}(\sigma_{1})\colon\lambda_{h\!f,!}\circ s^{\ast}\longrightarrow\delta_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\lambda_{f,!}

for the Beck-Chevalley transformation associated to the square
\sigma_{1}
, and define similarly
\mathsf{BC}(\sigma_{2})
and
\mathsf{BC}(\sigma_{12})
.
Unwinding
3.19
and under the equivalences
\alpha
and
\beta
above, we are called to prove that the diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{h\!f,!}\circ\pi_{h\!f}^{\ast}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f,!}\circ\pi_{f}^{\ast}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathsf{A},!}\circ\pi_{\mathsf{A}}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{BC}(\sigma_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{BC}(\sigma_{12})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{BC}(\sigma_{2})}

is canonically commutative.
Since
\sigma_{12}
is the composite square of
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
, this follows from the well-known componibility property of Beck-Chevalley transformations.
∎

Remark 3.25
.

The above proposition is just a shadow of the actual functorial dependence of
\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}
on the pair
(\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A})
.
To make this construction into an actual functor requires, as clearly shown by the above proof, to use the
(\infty,2)
-functoriality property of the Beck-Chevalley transformation.
Since this is not needed for the current paper, we leave the details for the interested reader.

Corollary 3.26
.

In the same setting of
3.23
, the triangle

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\mathcal{E}}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\Psi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}\circ\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathsf{C}}\circ\delta_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}\circ\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\psi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{h\!f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{h}}

is canonically commutative.

Proof.

We consider the case where
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{Y}
and
\mathcal{Z}
are presentable, as every other case is dealt with similarly.
We start with the following diagram:

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{A}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{B}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\mathsf{C}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{Z}}),\mathcal{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\mathcal{X},\mathsf{A}}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\mathcal{Y},\mathsf{B}}^{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{Z},\mathsf{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{\mathbf{1}}^{\ast}}

It is made
2
-commutative by the transformations
\phi_{f}
,
\phi_{h}
.

3.23
identifies their composite
\phi_{f}\circ(\phi_{h}\circ f_{\mathcal{E}})
with
\phi_{h\!f}
.
Since
\psi_{f}=\mathsf{BC}(\phi_{f})
,
\psi_{h}=\mathsf{BC}(\phi_{h})
and
\psi_{h\!f}=\mathsf{BC}(\phi_{h\!f})
, the conclusion follows directly from the componibility property of the Beck-Chevalley transformations.
∎

3.6.
Monodromy, revisited

Let us explain how to recover the monodromy equivalence as a particular instance of
3.11
.
Let
X
be a topological space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
From
[
HPT
, Construction 2.1]
, we have a functor

\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\ .

When
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{S}
is the
\infty
-category of spaces, this functor – denoted simply by
\Pi_{\infty}
– is the left Kan extension of the homotopy type functor, sending an open
U\in\mathrm{Open}(X)
to its homotopy type
\Pi_{\infty}(U)
.
For a general
\mathcal{E}
, we have a canonical identification
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\Pi_{\infty}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
As observed in
loc. cit.
,
