\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}
has a right adjoint

\Pi^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\ .

Assume now that
X
is locally weakly contractible.
Then,
[
HPT
, Proposition 2.5]
implies that
\Pi^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
is canonically identified with the constant hypersheaf functor
\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}
.

If
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{S}
, the functor
\Pi_{\infty}
can be canonically lifted to a functor

\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{/\Pi_{\infty}(X)}\ ,

which in turn admits a right adjoint
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
sending
f\colon K\to\Pi_{\infty}(X)
to the hypersheaf defined by

(3.27)

\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}(K)(U)\coloneqq\Map_{/\Pi_{\infty}(X)}(\Pi_{\infty}(U),K)\ .

Put
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}:=\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}
and denote by
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}

the right adjoint of
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}
.

Lemma 3.28
.

Let
X
be a weakly contractible and locally weakly contractible topological space.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Then,
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
factors through the full subcategory
\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

Since
X
is weakly contractible,
\Pi_{\infty}(X)\simeq*
, and therefore
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}
identifies with
\Pi_{\infty}
.
By tensoring with
\mathcal{E}
, we deduce that
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}
identifies with
\Pi_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}
.
Hence,
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
identifies with
\Pi^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Since
X
is locally weakly contractible,
[
HPT
, Proposition 2.5]
supplies a canonical identification of
\Pi^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
with
\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
The conclusion follows.
∎

Lemma 3.29
.

Let
X
be a locally weakly contractible topological space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Then
\Phi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}
and
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}
are canonically equivalent as functors from
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
to
\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}(X),\mathcal{E})
.
In particular,
\Psi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}
and
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}}
are canonically equivalent.

Proof.

It is enough to show that
\Phi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}
and
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}
are canonically equivalent.
From
3.8
, we have
\Phi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\simeq\Phi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Since
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{E}}=\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}
, we are left to show that
\Phi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}
are canonically equivalent.
To do this, it is enough to show that
\Psi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
are canonically equivalent.
Under the straightening equivalence
\mathcal{S}_{/\Pi_{\infty}(X)}\simeq\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}(X),\mathcal{S})
, the formula
Eq.

3.27
for
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
identifies canonically with the formula
Eq.

3.7
for
\Psi^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{X}
.
Thus
\Psi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
are canonically equivalent.
∎

Corollary 3.30
.

Let
X
be a locally weakly contractible topological space.
Then for every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
the functor
\Psi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\colon\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}(X),\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
factors through the full subcategory
\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

Let
F\colon\Pi_{\infty}(X)\to\mathcal{E}
be a functor.
Since
X
is locally weakly contractible, it is enough to prove that for every weakly contractible open subset
U
of
X
the restriction
\Psi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}(F)|_{U}
is locally hyperconstant.
Using
3.21
in the form of
3.22
-(2), we find a canonical equivalence

\Psi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}(F)|_{U}\simeq\Psi_{U}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}(F|_{\Pi_{\infty}(U)}).

Hence, we are left to suppose that
X
is weakly contractible and locally weakly contractible.
In that case,
3.30
follows from the combination of
3.29
and
3.28
.
∎

In particular,
3.30
implies that the adjunction
\Phi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\dashv\Psi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}
restricts to an adjunction

(3.31)

\Phi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\leftrightarrows\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}(X),\mathcal{E})\colon\Psi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\ .

At this point, we have:

Corollary 3.32
.

Let
X
be a locally weakly contractible topological space.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Then the adjunction (
3.31
) is an equivalence.

Proof.

Combine
3.29
with the monodromy equivalence
[
HPT
, Corollary 3.9]
.
∎

4.
Exodromy adjunction for constructible hypersheaves

Fix a conically stratified space
(X,P)
.
We specialize the discussion of
Section

3
to the context of
3.1
-(2).
In other words, we take
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
and consider the functor

\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}

left Kan extended from the functor sending an open
U
of
X
to the
\infty
-category of exit paths
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P|_{U})
.
Van Kampen’s theorem for exit paths (see
[
HA
, Theorem A.7.1]
) shows that
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}
is a colimit preserving functor.
Thus,
3.11
provides for every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
an adjunction

\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\leftrightarrows\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})\colon\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\ ,

which we refer to as the exodromy adjunction.
When the stratification on
X
is trivial, Corollaries
3.30
and
3.32
show that this adjunction restricts to an equivalence between
\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})
and the
\infty
-category of locally hyperconstant hypersheaves on
X
.
Our goal in this section is to show that the functor
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}
factors through the full subcategory
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
of constructible hypersheaves on
X
, and therefore that the exodromy adjunction restricts to an adjunction

\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\leftrightarrows\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})\colon\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\ .

Since the case of trivial stratification is already known, the main point is to show that the functor
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}
is compatible with the restriction to strata.

Throughout this section, we fix a presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
.
To lighten the notation, we will suppress the
\mathcal{E}
superscript in the exodromy adjunction.

4.1.
Criteria for
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
to be an equivalence

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces.

3.19
associated to
f
a natural transformation

\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\ .

Our goal is to prove that
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence when
f
is the inclusion of a single stratum.
For this, we start laying out general locality properties of
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
.

Notation 4.1
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces.
For every open
U\subseteq X
we let
Y_{U}\coloneqq U\times_{X}Y
and we denote by
f_{U}\colon(Y_{U},Q)\to(U,P)
the induced morphism.

The formation of the hypersheaf associated to a functor from the Exit Paths is local.
This is justified by the following

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
j:U\to X
be the inclusion of an open subset of
X
.
Then, the natural transformation

\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Psi_{U,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(j)^{\ast}\

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Since
j:U\to X
is the inclusion of an open subset of
X
, the functor
j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}:\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(U;\mathcal{E})
is computed as the presheaf pullback
j^{-1}
.
On the other hand,
j^{-1}
admits a fully-faithul left adjoint
j_{!}
.
Hence,
j_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}:=\mathrm{hyp}\circ j_{!}
is left adjoint to
j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}
.
Since the hypersheafification commutes with
j^{-1}
, the unit transformation
\mathrm{id}\to j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ j_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence.
Then,
4.2
follows from
3.21
.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces.
Let
i:U\to X
be the inclusion of an open subset of
X
.
Let
j:f^{-1}(U)\to Y
be the induced inclusion.
Then, for every
F\in\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})
, the transformation
j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F))
is canonically equivalent to

\psi_{f_{U}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i)^{\ast}(F))
.

Proof.

Apply
3.26
and
4.2
twice.
∎

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces.
Let
\mathcal{B}
be a basis for the topology of
X
.
Assume that for every
U\in\mathcal{B}
, the transformation

\psi_{f_{U}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon f_{U}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Psi_{U,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Psi_{Y_{U},Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f_{U})^{\ast}

is an equivalence.
Then,
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Fix
F\in\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})
.
We have to show that
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
is an equivalence of hypersheaves on
Y
.
This statement is local on
Y
.
In particular, for
U\in\mathcal{B}
, if
i:U\to X
and
j:f^{-1}(U)\to Y
are the inclusions, it is enough to show that
j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F))
is an equivalence.
Then,
4.4
follows from
4.3
.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces.
Then,
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is canonically equivalent to

\mathrm{hyp}(\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}})
.

Proof.

Apply
3.26
and
3.18
-
(2)
twice.
∎

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces.
Fix
F\in\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})
.
Assume that the collection
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}(f,F)
of open subsets
V\subseteq Y
for which the transformation

\psi^{\mathrm{psh}}_{f}(F)(V)\colon f^{-1}\big(\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F)\big)(V)\to\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{psh}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}(F)\big)(V)

is an equivalence is a basis for the topology of
Y
.
Then,
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let
j\colon\mathcal{B}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Open}(Y)
be the canonical inclusion.
Consider the commutative square

(4.7)

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\big(\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F)\big)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{psh}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}(F)\big)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}j^{-1}f^{-1}\big(\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F)\big)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}j^{-1}\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{psh}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}(F)\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{\mathrm{psh}}_{f}(F)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}j^{-1}\psi^{\mathrm{psh}}_{f}(F)}

From
3.18
, the presheaf
\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{psh}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}(F)\big)
is a hypersheaf on
Y
.
From
[
HPT
, Proposition 1.13]
, the adjunction
j^{-1}\dashv j_{*}
induces an equivalence between
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})
and the full subcategory
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{B};\mathcal{E})
of
\mathrm{PSh}(\mathcal{B};\mathcal{E})
spanned by the functors
F:\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{E}
such that
j_{*}(F)
is a hypersheaf on
Y
.
Hence, the right vertical arrow of
Eq.

4.7
is an equivalence.
Since
j^{-1}\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F)
is an equivalence by assumption, the bottom arrow is an equivalence as well.
Thus,
j_{*}j^{-1}f^{-1}\big(\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F)\big)
is a hypersheaf on
Y
.
Then,
[
HPT
, Remark 1.14]
implies that the left vertical arrow exhibits its target as the hypersheafification of its source.
Hence,

j_{*}j^{-1}\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F)\simeq\mathrm{hyp}(\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F))\simeq\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)

where the last equivalence follows from
4.5
.

4.6
thus follows.
∎

Corollary 4.8
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a morphism between locally weakly contractible topological spaces.
Then for every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
, both transformations

\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Psi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Psi_{Y}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}(f)^{\ast}\quad\text{and}\quad\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Phi_{Y}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}(f)^{\ast}\circ\Phi_{X}^{\mathrm{hyp}}

are equivalences once restricted to locally hyperconstant hypersheaves.

Proof.

Combining
3.20
-(1) and
3.32
we see that it is enough to show that
\psi_{f}
is an equivalence.
Using
4.4
, we can reduce ourselves to the case where
X
is weakly contractible, so that the conclusion follows from
3.29
and
[
HPT
, Proposition 2.5]
.
∎

4.2.
Restriction to strata for
\Psi

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
a\in P
and let
i_{a}\colon X_{a}\to X
be the inclusion of the associated stratum.
Then the natural transformation

\psi_{a}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Psi_{X_{a}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i_{a})^{\ast}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be the poset of open subsets
U
of
X_{a}
such that the set
\mathcal{B}^{\exc}(U)
of open neighbourhoods
V
of
U
in
X
where
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,\{a\})\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V,P)
is final is a fundamental system of open neighbourhoods of
U
in
X
.
From
2.20
, the set

\mathcal{B}
is a basis of
X_{a}
.
Fix
F\in\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})
.
From
4.6
, we are left to show that for every

U\in\mathcal{B}
, the morphism

\psi^{\mathrm{psh}}_{i_{a}}(F)(U)\colon i_{a}^{-1}\big(\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F)\big)(U)\to\Psi_{X_{a},\{a\}}^{\mathrm{psh}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i_{a})^{\ast}(F)\big)(U)

is an equivalence.
On the one hand

i_{a}^{-1}(\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F))(U)\simeq\colim_{V\in\mathcal{B}^{\exc}(U)}\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F)(V)\simeq\colim_{V\in\mathcal{B}^{\exc}(U)}\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V,P)}F|_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V,P)}\ .

Since for every
V\in\mathcal{B}^{\exc}(U)
, the functor
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,\{a\})\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V,P)
is final, we deduce

i_{a}^{-1}(\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}(F))(U)\simeq\colim_{V\in\mathcal{B}^{\exc}(U)}\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,\{a\})}F|_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,\{a\})}\simeq\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,\{a\})}F|_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,\{a\})}\ .

On the other hand,

\Psi_{X_{a}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i_{a})^{\ast}(F)\big)(U)\simeq\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,\{a\})}F|_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,\{a\})}\ .

This concludes the proof of
4.9
.
∎

Corollary 4.10
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Then the functor
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
factors through the full subcategory
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of
4.9
and
3.30
.
∎

5.
The exodromy equivalence

5.1.
A criterion for
\phi_{f}
to be an equivalence

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
As usual
\mathcal{E}
will be fixed throughout this section, and we therefore suppress it from the notations.
Our first goal is to provide a general method to establish when the natural transformation

\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Phi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\circ\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}

is an equivalence.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces.
Then the following statements hold;

(1)

the transformations
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}
and

\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\mathrm{hyp}
are canonically equivalent.

(2)

for every diagram
F:I\to\mathrm{PSh}(X;\mathcal{E})
with
I
a small
\infty
-category, the canonical morphism

\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}(\colim_{i\in I}j(F_{i}))\longrightarrow\colim_{i\in I}\phi_{f}(F_{i})

is an equivalence, where
j:\mathrm{PSh}(X;\mathcal{E})\to\Fun(\mathrm{PSh}(X;\mathcal{E})^{\mathrm{op}};\mathcal{E})
is the Yoneda embedding.

Proof.

For the first point, apply
3.18
-
(2)
and
3.23
twice.
For the second point, we have

\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}(\colim_{i\in I}j(F_{i}))\simeq\colim_{i\in I}\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}(j(F_{i}))\simeq\colim_{i\in I}\phi_{f}(F_{i})

where the first equivalence follows from the fact that
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}
is built out of left adjoint functors and where the second equivalence follows from the observation that
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}
identifies with the left Kan extension of
\Phi_{X,P}
along the Yoneda embedding.
∎

Corollary 5.2
.

If
\phi_{f}
is an equivalence, the same goes for
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{psh}}
and
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
.

Proof.

The first statement comes from
5.1
-
(2)
and the fact that every presheaf is a small colimit of representable objects.
The second statements then follows from the observation from
5.1
-
(1)
.
∎

The advantage of
\phi_{f}
is that it can be explicitly computed in terms of open subsets of
Y
, rather than having to deal with all hypersheaves on
Y
.
To fully exploit this, let us specialize the main construction of
Section

3.4
to the current setting.

Notation 5.3
.

For a conically stratified space
(X,P)
, let

\pi_{X}\colon\mathrm{E}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}

be the cartesian fibration classifying the exit paths
\infty
-functor
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}\colon\mathrm{Open}(X)\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
We let

\lambda_{X}\colon\mathrm{E}_{X}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)

be the canonical localization functor.
Given a morphism
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
of conically stratified spaces, we consider the following diagram:

(5.4)

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(Y,Q)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{Y}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{f}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{X}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Open}(Y)^{\mathrm{op}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}}

With these notations,
3.18
-(1) shows that
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}\simeq\pi_{X,\ast}\circ\lambda_{X}^{\ast}
.
In turn, we obtain:

Corollary 5.5
.

(1)

There is a canonical identification
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}\simeq\lambda_{X,!}\circ\pi_{X}^{\ast}
.

(2)

The Beck-Chevalley transformation

(f^{-1})^{\ast}\circ\pi_{Y,\ast}\longrightarrow\pi_{f,\ast}\circ q^{\ast}

is an equivalence.

(3)

If the Beck-Chevalley transformation

\lambda_{f,!}\circ p^{\ast}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\circ\lambda_{X,!}

is an equivalence, then so is
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
.

Proof.

Point (1) follows from the identification
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{psh}}\simeq\pi_{X,\ast}\circ\lambda_{X}^{\ast}
passing to left adjoints.
Point (2) is just a reformulation of
3.15
in this specific situation.
As for point (3), observe that by construction
\phi_{f}
is the composition

\lambda_{Y,!}\circ\pi_{Y}^{\ast}\circ(f^{-1})_{!}\simeq\lambda_{f,!}\circ\pi_{f}^{\ast}\simeq\lambda_{f,!}\circ p^{\ast}\circ\pi_{X}^{\ast}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\circ\lambda_{X,!}\circ\pi_{X}^{\ast}\ .

Therefore, if the Beck-Chevalley transformation of the statement is an equivalence, the same goes for
\phi_{f}
.
Thus, the conclusion follows from
5.2
.
∎

5.2.
Restriction to a closed union of strata for
\Phi

Assume that
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
is the inclusion of a closed union of strata.
The following lemma is a straightforward consequence of Quillen’s theorem A and its proof is left to the reader:

Lemma 5.6
.

Let

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{0}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}_{0}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

be a pullback square in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
Assume that:

(1)

the functors
i
and
j
are fully faithful;

(2)

for
d\in\mathcal{D}
,
d_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{0}
, if
\Map_{\mathcal{D}}(d,j(d_{0}))\neq\emptyset
, then
d
belongs to the essential image of
j
.

Then, for any
\mathcal{E}
presentable
\infty
-category the Beck-Chevalley transformation
g_{!}\circ i^{*}\to j^{*}\circ f_{!}
is an equivalence.

Corollary 5.7
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
S\subset P
be a closed downwards subset and let
f\colon X_{S}\to X
be the inclusion of the corresponding closed union of strata.
The upper right square of the diagram (
5.4
)

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{f}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{X}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X_{S},S)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

induces the Beck-Chevalley transformation

\beta\colon\lambda_{f!}\circ p^{*}\longrightarrow f^{*}\circ\lambda_{X!},

which is an equivalence.

Proof.

We check that the conditions of
5.6
are satisfied.
First of all we observe that for every open subset
U
of
X
, one has
U\cap X_{S}\simeq U_{S}
, where we consider
U
equipped with the induced stratification
P|_{U}
.
From
2.6
, the functor

f_{U}\colon\mathrm{Exit}(U_{S},S|_{U})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Exit}(U,P|_{U})

is fully faithful.
It follows that both
f
and
p
are fully faithful functors.
That the above square is a pullback is then obvious.
Finally, the condition (3) from
5.6
holds since
S\subset P
is closed downwards.
∎

Combining Corollaries
5.5
-(3) and
5.7
we immediately obtain the following:

Corollary 5.8
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
S\subset P
be a closed downwards subset and let
f\colon X_{S}\to X
be the inclusion of the corresponding closed union of strata.
Then the natural transformation

\phi_{S}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Phi_{X_{S},S}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\circ\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}

is an equivalence.

5.3.
Restriction to an open union of strata for
\Phi

Dealing with the inclusion of an open union of strata is more complicated.
We start collecting some general
\infty
-categorical facts:

Definition 5.9
.

We say that a functor
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is weakly cofiltered if for every object
d\in\mathcal{D}
the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{d/}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{D}_{d/}
is cofiltered.

Remark 5.10
.

If
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is cofiltered in the sense of
3.4
it is also weakly cofiltered: indeed,
3.5
shows that for every
d\in\mathcal{D}
the induced map
\mathcal{C}_{d/}\to\mathcal{D}_{d/}
is cofiltered and, on the other hand,
\mathcal{D}_{d/}
is obviously cofiltered since it has an initial object.
On the other hand, Quillen’s theorem A implies that if
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is weakly cofiltered, then it is also colimit-final.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-category.
Let
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
be functors and let

\pi_{\mathsf{A}}\colon\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\ ,\qquad\pi_{\mathsf{B}}\colon\mathcal{B}\longrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}

be the associated cartesian fibrations.
Let
f\colon\mathsf{A}\to\mathsf{B}
be a natural transformation.
If for every
x\in\mathcal{X}
the functor
f_{x}\colon\mathsf{A}(x)\to\mathsf{B}(x)
is weakly cofiltered, then the induced functor
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is colimit-final.

Proof.

Let
b\in\mathcal{B}
be an object.
In virtue of Quillen’s theorem A, we have to prove that the
\infty
-category

\mathcal{A}_{b/}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}_{b/}

is weakly contractible.
We claim that it is cofiltered.
Set
x\coloneqq\pi_{\mathsf{B}}(b)
, so that we can review
b
as an element in
\mathcal{B}_{x}\simeq\mathsf{B}(x)
.
Write
\mathsf{A}(x)_{b/}\coloneqq\mathsf{A}(x)\times_{\mathsf{B}(x)}\mathsf{B}(x)_{b/}
and let

j\colon\mathsf{A}(x)_{b/}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{b/}

be the natural functor.
Let now
F\colon I\to\mathcal{A}_{b/}
be a finite diagram.
Composing
F
with the canonical projection
\mathcal{A}_{b/}\to\mathcal{X}_{x/}
and using the fact that both
\pi_{\mathsf{A}}
and
\pi_{\mathsf{B}}
are cartesian fibrations, we deduce the existence of a functor

F^{\prime}\colon I\to\mathsf{A}(x)_{b/}\coloneqq\mathsf{A}(x)\times_{\mathsf{B}(x)}\mathsf{B}(x)_{b/}

together with a natural transformation
\gamma\colon j\circ F^{\prime}\to F
.
By assumption the functor
f_{x}\colon\mathsf{A}(x)\to\mathsf{B}(x)
is weakly cofiltered, and therefore
\mathsf{A}(x)_{b/}
is cofiltered.
It follows that
F^{\prime}
admits an extension
\widetilde{F}^{\prime}\colon I^{\lhd}\to\mathsf{A}(x)_{b/}
.
At this point, the natural transformation
\gamma
allows us to prolong
\widetilde{F}^{\prime}
into an extension
\widetilde{F}\colon I^{\lhd}\to\mathcal{A}_{b/}
of
F
.
The conclusion follows.
∎

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Fix an object
x\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
.
Define
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)_{/x}^{\simeq}
as the full subcategory of
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)_{/x}
spanned by its final objects.
For every functor
\mathcal{C}\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
we set

\mathcal{C}_{/x}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}\times_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)_{/x}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{C}_{/x}^{\simeq}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}\times_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)_{/x}^{\simeq}\ .

The following lemma contains the key geometrical argument of this section:

Lemma 5.12
.

The functor

(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}^{\simeq}\longrightarrow(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}

is cofinal.

Proof.

We apply
5.11
taking
\mathcal{X}=\mathrm{Open}(X)
, and
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{B}
to be the functors given by

\mathsf{A}(U)\coloneqq\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}^{\simeq}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{B}(U)\coloneqq\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}\ .

By construction, the cartesian fibration classified by
\mathsf{B}
is given by the natural projection
(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}\to\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}
, and similarly the cartesian fibration classified by
\mathsf{A}
is given by
(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}^{\simeq}\to\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}
.
Thus,
5.11
shows that it is enough to show that for every open
U
of
X
, the functor

\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}^{\simeq}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}

is weakly cofiltered.
Let
\mathbf{\gamma}\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}
and write
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{\mathbf{\gamma}/\!\!/x}:=(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x})_{\mathbf{\gamma}/}
.
We show that the
\infty
-category

\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{\mathbf{\gamma}/\!\!/x}^{\simeq}\coloneqq\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}^{\simeq}\times_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}}\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{\mathbf{\gamma}/\!\!/x}

is cofiltered.
Let therefore
I
be a finite category and consider the lifting problem

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{\mathbf{\gamma}/\!\!/x}^{\simeq}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\lhd}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{g}}

Since
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{\mathbf{\gamma}/\!\!/x}^{\simeq}
is fully faithful inside
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{\mathbf{\gamma}/\!\!/x}
, we can rewrite the above lifting problem as

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{\mathbf{\gamma}/\!\!/x}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\lhd}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{g}}

where
g
sends an object
i\in I
to a morphism
\mathbf{\gamma}\to\mathbf{\delta}_{i}
in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}
with
\mathbf{\delta}_{i}\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}^{\simeq}
and where
\overline{g}
is required to send the vertex
v_{0}
of
I^{\lhd}
to a morphism
\mathbf{\gamma}\to\mathbf{\delta}
in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}
with
\mathbf{\delta}\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}^{\simeq}
.
This is equivalent to a lifting problem

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\lhd}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(I^{\lhd})^{\lhd}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\lhd}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{g}^{\prime}}

where
g^{\prime}
sends the vertex
v_{1}
of the exterior cone
(I^{\lhd})^{\lhd}
to
\mathbf{\gamma}
and the other objects in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}^{\simeq}
, and where
\overline{g}^{\prime}
is required to send
v_{0}
in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)_{/x}^{\simeq}
.
Unraveling the definitions and denoting by
v_{2}
the final object of
((I^{\lhd})^{\lhd})^{\rhd}
, we can further reduce this lifting problem to the following one

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(I^{\lhd})^{\rhd}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((I^{\lhd})^{\lhd})^{\rhd}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\lhd}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{h}}

where we ask for
\overline{h}
to represent a continuous morphism

\overline{\mathrm{h}}\colon|((I^{\lhd})^{\lhd})^{\rhd}|\longrightarrow X,

with the following properties:

(1)

the morphism
\mathrm{h}
takes the segment
[v_{1},v_{2}]
to the exit path
\gamma
underlying
\mathbf{\gamma}
.

(2)

the morphism
\mathrm{h}
takes the complement of
v_{1}
to the same stratum of
x
;

(3)

the morphism
\mathrm{h}
takes the double cone
(I^{\lhd})^{\lhd}
inside the open
U
.

Observe that there is a canonical equivalence

((I^{\lhd})^{\lhd})^{\rhd}\simeq(\Delta^{1}\star I)\star\Delta^{0}\simeq\Delta^{1}\star(I\star\Delta^{0})\simeq((I^{\rhd})^{\lhd})^{\lhd}\ ,

where
\star
denotes the join operation.
Set
J\coloneqq I^{\rhd}
.
By definition of
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
, we can represent
h
by an explicit continuous morphism

\mathrm{h}\colon|J^{\lhd}|\longrightarrow X,

with the property that it takes the segment
[v_{1},v_{2}]
to
\gamma
, that the complement of
v_{1}
is sent in the same stratum as
x
, and that the cone over
I
with vertex
v_{1}
is sent in
U
.
Since
\mathrm{h}
is continuous, the preimage of
U
is an open of
|J^{\lhd}|
containing the cone over
I
with vertex
v_{1}
.
Then, the inclusion of the exterior cone

i\coloneqq|j^{\lhd}|\colon|J^{\lhd}|\hookrightarrow|(J^{\lhd})^{\lhd}|

admits a retraction
r
sending
v_{0}
inside
[v_{1},v_{2}]
close enough to
v_{1}
and satisfying the following extra properties:

(i)

r
sends
|(I^{\lhd})^{\lhd}|
inside
\mathrm{h}^{-1}(U)
;

(ii)

the vertex
v_{1}
is the unique point
s\in|(J^{\lhd})^{\lhd}|
such that
r(s)=v_{1}
.

Define

\overline{\mathrm{h}}\coloneqq\mathrm{h}\circ r.

It is then straightforward to verify that
\overline{\mathrm{h}}
satisfies the conditions (1),(2) and (3) listed above.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
S\subset P
be a closed upwards subset and let
f\colon X_{S}\to X
be the inclusion of the corresponding open union of strata.
The upper right square of the diagram (
5.4
)

(5.14)

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{f}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{E}_{X}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X_{S},S)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

induces the Beck-Chevalley transformation

\beta\colon\lambda_{f!}\circ p^{*}\longrightarrow f^{*}\circ\lambda_{X!}

which is an equivalence.

Proof.

As in the proof of
5.7
,
f
and
p
are fully faithful and the above square is a pullback.
Let now
x\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X_{S},S)
be an object.
Committing a slight abuse of notation, we still denote by
x
its image in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
via the functor
f
.
Put

(\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x}\coloneqq\mathrm{E}_{f}\times_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X_{S},S)}\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X_{S},S)_{/x}

and consider the commutative diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(}\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X_{S},S)_{/x}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)_{/x}.}

Unraveling the definitions, we reduce ourselves to check that
(\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x}\longrightarrow(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}
is cofinal.
Observe that since
p
is fully faithful, the same goes for this map.
By definition,
(\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x}
is the full subcategory of triples
(U,y,\gamma)
in
(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}
such that
y
lies in
X_{S}
.
Using the same notations of
5.12
, we find the following commutative diagram of
\infty
-categories over
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)_{/x}
:

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x}^{\simeq}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{(}\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}^{\simeq}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}.}

Since the vertical functors and the bottom horizontal one are fully faithful, the same goes for the top horizontal one.
Furthermore, if
(U,y,\gamma)\in(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}^{\simeq}
then
\gamma\colon y\to x
is an equivalence in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
.
Since
x
belongs to
X_{S}
, the same goes for
y
.
In other words,
(U,y,\gamma)
belongs to
(\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x}^{\simeq}
.
This shows that the top horizontal arrow is also essentially surjective, hence an equivalence.
As a consequence, to prove that the bottom horizontal functor is cofinal it is enough to prove that both vertical functors are cofinal.

We now show that it is enough to prove that the right vertical functor is cofinal.
By Quillen’s Theorem A and the above discussion, it is enough to show that for
\mathbf{y}=(U,y,\gamma)\in(\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x}
, the fully faithful functor
((\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x})_{\mathbf{y}/}\to((\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x})_{\mathbf{y}/}
is an equivalence.
Let
\mathbf{z}\coloneqq(V,z,\delta)\in(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}
and let
\mathbf{y}\to\mathbf{z}
be a morphism in
(\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x}
.
Observe that this morphism produces an exit path
y\to z
.
Since
S
is closed upwards, this implies that
z
lies in
X_{S}
. Thus, we have
\mathbf{z}\in(\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x}
.
Hence, the fully faithful functor
((\mathrm{E}_{f})_{/x})_{\mathbf{y}/}\to((\mathrm{E}_{X})_{/x})_{\mathbf{y}/}
is an equivalence and the sought-after reduction is complete.
We then conclude the proof of
5.13
using
5.12
.
∎

Combining Corollaries
5.5
-(3) and
5.13
we immediately obtain the following:

Corollary 5.15
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
S\subset P
be a closed upwards subset and let
f\colon X_{S}\to X
be the inclusion of the corresponding open union of strata.
Then the natural transformation

\phi_{S}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Phi_{X_{S},S}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\circ\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}

is an equivalence.

Corollary 5.16
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
a\in P
and let
i_{a}\colon X_{a}\to X
be the inclusion of the corresponding stratum.
Then the natural transformation

\phi_{a}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Phi_{X_{a}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i_{a})^{\ast}\circ\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

It follows by first applying first
5.15
to
(X,P)
and the subset
P_{\geqslant a}\subseteq P
and subsequently
5.8
to
(X_{\geqslant a},P_{\geqslant a})
and the subset
\{a\}\subseteq P_{\geqslant a}
.
∎

5.4.
The equivalence

We are now ready for the main theorem:

Theorem 5.17
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Assume that:

(1)

for every
a\in P
, the associated stratum
X_{a}
is locally weakly contractible;

(2)

the hyper-restrictions

\big\{i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{a};\mathcal{E})\big\}_{a\in P}

are jointly conservative.

Then the exodromy adjunction

\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\leftrightarrows\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})\colon\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We have to prove that for every functor
F\colon\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)\to\mathcal{E}
and every hyperconstructible hypersheaf
G\in\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
, the unit and counit

\eta_{G}\colon G\longrightarrow\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(G))\quad\text{and}\quad\varepsilon_{F}\colon\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F))\longrightarrow F

are equivalences.
By assumption, it is enough to check that for every
a\in P
, the (hyper-)restrictions
i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(\eta_{G})
and
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i_{a})^{\ast}(\varepsilon_{F})
are equivalences.
Consider the following square:

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E}\big)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{a};\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}(X_{a}),\mathcal{E}\big)\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i_{a})^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{X_{a}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}}

5.16
shows that the natural transformation
\phi_{a}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
makes this into a commutative diagram.
Moreover, combining
3.20
-(2) and
4.9
we deduce that it is horizontally right adjointable.
Therefore we obtain canonical identifications

i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(\eta_{G})\simeq\eta_{i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(G)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i_{a})^{\ast}(\varepsilon_{F})\simeq\varepsilon_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i_{a})^{\ast}(F)}\ ,

where the latter are the unit and the counit of the adjunction
\Phi_{X_{a}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\dashv\Psi_{X_{a}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
.
Observe now that since
G
is hyperconstructible,
i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(G)
is locally constant.
Thus the conclusion follows directly from
3.32
.
∎

Remark 5.18
.

The joint conservativity of the hyper-restrictions to strata is satisfied in the following two cases of interest:

(1)

the category
\mathcal{E}
is compactly generated.
See
[
HPT
, Corollary 5.16]
.

(2)

the poset
P
is noetherian and the category
\mathcal{E}
is stable and presentable.
See
[
HPT
, Corollary 5.21]
.

Let us emphasize some immediate consequences of the above theorem.

Corollary 5.19
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
If the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, then:

(1)

the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
is presentable;

(2)

if
\mathcal{E}
is an
\infty
-topos, then so is
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

The following corollary generalizes at the same time
[
HA
, Lemma A.9.14]
and
[
HPT
, Corollary 3.2]

Corollary 5.20
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
If the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, then the full subcategory
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
is closed under small limits and small colimits.

Proof.

For colimits it is enough to observe that if
a\in P
and if
i_{a}\colon X_{a}\to X
is the inclusion of the corresponding stratum, then
i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{a};\mathcal{E})
commutes with colimits.
Thus, we are reduced to show that
\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
is closed under colimits in
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
This was shown in
[
HPT
, Corollary 3.2]
.
Concerning limits, it is enough to observe that
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
is fully faithful and right adjoint to
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
.
Thus
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
commutes with small limits.
On the other hand
5.17
shows that the essential image of
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
, whence the conclusion.
∎

Remark 5.21
.

(1)

Combining Corollaries
5.19
and
5.20
it follows that if the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied then the inclusion
\iota:\mathrm{Cons}_{P}(X;\mathcal{E})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
admits both a left adjoint
\mathrm{L}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
and a right adjoint
\mathrm{R}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
.

(2)

The functor

\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}:\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})

factors as
\iota\circ\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
.
Hence, if the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, it admits both
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}|_{\mathrm{Cons}_{P}(X;\mathcal{E})}\circ\mathrm{L}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
as left adjoints.
Thus, for every
F\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
, the unit adjunction
F\to\iota\circ L_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
induces an equivalence
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\simeq\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}L_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
.

Corollary 5.22
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Under the assumptions of
5.17
, for a hypersheaf
F\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

F
is hyperconstructible on
(X,P)
;

(2)

for every open inclusion
U\subset V
for which the induced map
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V,P)
is a categorical equivalence, the restriction map
F(V)\to F(U)
is an equivalence;

(3)

for every conical chart
Z\times C(Y)
of
(X,P)
,
F
satisfies the following two conditions:

(i)

for every open subset
W\subset Z
and every
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon^{\prime}\leqslant 1
, the restriction map

F(W\times C_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(Y))\longrightarrow F(W\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y))

is an equivalence;

(ii)

for every inclusion
U\subseteq V
of weakly contractible open subsets of
Z
, the restriction map

F(V\times C(Y))\longrightarrow F(U\times C(Y))

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The implication (1)
\Rightarrow
(2) is a direct consequence of
5.17
.
For the implication (2)
\Rightarrow
(3), it is enough to observe that, on the one hand, the inclusion
W\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y)\hookrightarrow W\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y)
is a stratified homotopy equivalence (and hence it satisfies the assumption of point (2)); and on the other hand, for an inclusion
U\subseteq V
of weakly contractible open subsets of
Z
, the induced map
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U\times C(Y))\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V\times C(Y))
is also a categorical equivalence, thanks to
2.14
.
We are therefore left to prove that (3) implies (1).
The question is local, so we can replace
X
by a conical chart of the form
Z\times C(Y)
.
Letting
i\colon Z\to Z\times C(Y)
be the natural inclusion, we only have to check that
i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
is locally hyperconstant.
Consider first the presheaf-theoretic pullback
i^{-1}(F)
.
For every open subset
W
of
Z
, one has

i^{-1}(F)(W)\simeq\colim_{0<\varepsilon\leqslant 1}F(W\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y))\ .

Our assumption (i) guarantees that the transition maps in the above colimit diagram are equivalences, and hence that the canonical restriction map

F(W\times C(Y))\longrightarrow i^{-1}(F)(W)

is an equivalence.
In particular, it follows that
i^{-1}(F)
is a hypersheaf, and therefore that it coincides with
i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
.

At this point, in order to prove that
i^{-1}(F)\simeq i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
is locally hyperconstant it is enough, in virtue of
[
HPT
, Proposition 3.1]
, to prove that for every inclusion
U\subset V
of weakly contractible open subsets of
Z
, the restriction map

i^{-1}(F)(V)\longrightarrow i^{-1}(F)(U)

is an equivalence.
As we showed above, this amounts to check that the restriction map

F(V\times C(Y))\longrightarrow F(U\times C(Y))

is an equivalence.
Since this holds by assumption (ii), the conclusion follows.
∎

For later use, let us remark that in the previous proof we established the following fact:

Corollary 5.23
.

Let
Z
be a locally weakly contractible topological space and let
(Y,Q)
be a stratified space so that
(X,P)\coloneqq(Z\times C(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})
is conically stratified.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category and assume that the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied by
(X,P)
and
\mathcal{E}
.
Let
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
and let
U
be an open subset of
Z
.
Then the canonical map

F(U\times C(Y))\longrightarrow i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)(U)

is an equivalence, where
i
denotes the canonical inclusion
i\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
.

6.
Consequences

Having proven
5.17
, we now explore some of its consequences.
In this section, we mainly focus on improved functoriality results for the exodromy equivalence and some structural results for the
\infty
-category of hyperconstructible hypersheaves.

6.1.
Structural results for hyperconstructible hypersheaves

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified with locally weakly contractible strata.
Applying
5.19
, we see that
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
is a presentable
\infty
-category.
In particular, for every
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
, the tensor product
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}
is well defined.
Our next task is to compare it with
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

Since
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
is presentable, it follows that the inclusion
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
is a morphism in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
.
Therefore, tensoring with
\mathcal{E}
yields a fully faithful functor

(6.1)

\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}\ .

Let
a\in P
and let
i_{a}\colon X_{a}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of the corresponding stratum.
Functoriality of the tensor product of presentable
\infty
-categories immediately implies the commutativity of the following diagram:

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{a})\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{a})\otimes\mathcal{E}\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}}

Recall from
[
HPT
, Observation 3.11]
that the equivalence
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{a})\otimes\mathcal{E}\simeq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{a};\mathcal{E})
restricts to an equivalence
\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{a})\otimes\mathcal{E}\simeq\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{a};\mathcal{E})
.
Thus it follows that under the equivalence
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}\simeq\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
, the functor (
6.1
) factors through
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
, yielding the following commutative diagram:

(6.2)

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\ .}

Corollary 6.3
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
If the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, then the diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{S}\big)\otimes\mathcal{E}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E}\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boxtimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boxtimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}}

is canonically commutative.
In particular, the left vertical functor is an equivalence.

Proof.

The first half is a direct consequence of
3.8
and the commutativity of the diagram (
6.2
).
The second half follows from the first one and the 2-out-of-3 property of the equivalences.
∎

Corollary 6.4
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
If the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, then the
\infty
-category
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
is
\kappa
-presentable.

Proof.

Indeed,
\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{S})
is always compactly generated, and therefore
\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{S})\otimes\mathcal{E}
is
\kappa
-presentable by
[
HA
, Lemma 5.3.2.11]
.
∎

Corollary 6.5

(Categorical Künneth formula)
.

Let
(X,P)
and
(Y,Q)
be conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata.
Then there is a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{Cons}_{P\times Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X\times Y)\simeq\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)\otimes\mathrm{Cons}_{Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y)\ .

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
\Fun(-,\mathcal{S})
takes products of
\infty
-categories to tensor products of presentable
\infty
-categories, and the fact that
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}
commutes with finite products of stratified spaces.
∎

6.2.
Exodromic morphisms

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Although the natural transformations

\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\quad\text{and}\quad\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Phi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\circ\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}

are always defined, they are not always equivalences.

Definition 6.6
.

We say that the morphism
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
is:

•

right exodromic
if the natural transformation
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence;

•

left exodromic
if the natural transformation
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence when evaluated on hyperconstructible hypersheaves on
(X,P)
;

•

strongly left exodromic
if the natural transformation
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence;

•

(
strongly
)
exodromic
if it is right and (strongly) left exodromic.

With this terminology and with the help of
5.17
,
3.20
-(2) implies:

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category satisfying the assumptions of
5.17
.
Then
f
is left exodromic if and only if it is right exodromic.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category satisfying the assumptions of
5.17
.
Then
f
is left exodromic.

Proof.

From
6.7
, it is enough to prove that
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence.
For this, it is enough to show that for every
a\in Q
, the hyper-restriction
i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}})
is an equivalence, where
i_{a}:Y_{a}\to Y
is the inclusion.
From
4.9
and
3.26
, we find for every
F:\Pi^{\Sigma}_{\infty}(X,P)\to\mathcal{E}
a canonical identification

i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F))\simeq\psi_{f\circ i_{a}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\ .

Let
b\in P
be the image of
a
, let
i_{b}:X_{b}\to X
be the inclusion and let
g:Y_{a}\to X_{b}
be the morphism morphism induced by
f
.
Then,
f\circ i_{a}=i_{b}\circ g
so that a second application of
4.9
and
3.26
gives a canonical identification

i_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F))\simeq\psi_{g}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\Pi^{\Sigma}_{\infty}(i_{b})^{\ast}(F))\ .

Thus, the conclusion follows from
4.8
.
∎

Remark 6.9
.

In particular, if
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
is an open immersion,
6.8
implies that
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence on hyperconstructible hypersheaves.
When
f
is the inclusion of an open union of strata of
(X,P)
, Corollaries
3.21
and
5.15
show that more is true: indeed, in this case
f
is strongly exodromic.

Corollary 6.10
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category satisfying the assumptions of
5.17
.
Let
x\in X
be a point and let
F\in\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
We have:

\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)(x)\simeq\colim_{x\in U}F(U)\ ,

where the colimit ranges over the open subsets of
X
containing
x
.

Proof.

Review
x
as a morphism
x\colon*\to X
.
The induced stratification on
\ast
is the trivial one, and in particular it is conical.
Thus,
6.8
implies that
x
is exodromic, and therefore that the natural transformation
\phi_{x}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence.
The conclusion follows since the colimit in the statement is canonically identified with
x^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
.
∎

The next lemma asserts that the failure for the formula from
6.10
to hold exactly measures the defect for a hypersheaf to be hyperconstructible.

Corollary 6.11
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category satisfying the assumptions of
5.17
.
Let
F\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
Then
F
is hyperconstructible on
(X,P)
if and only if for every
x\in X
, the canonical morphism

\phi_{x}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F):\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)(x)\longrightarrow\colim_{x\in U}F(U)\

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The direct implication follows from
6.10
.
Assume now that
\phi_{x}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
is an equivalence for every
x\in X
.
Let
L_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
be the left adjoint to the inclusion
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}(X;\mathcal{E})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
as obtained in
5.21
-
(2)
.
To prove
6.11
, it is enough to prove that the unit transformation
F\to L_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
is an equivalence.
Since both source and target are hypersheaves it is enough to show that for every
x\in X
, the induced morphism
F_{x}\to L_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)_{x}
is an equivalence.
By assumption,
F_{x}
identifies with
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)(x)
.
On the other hand,
6.10
combined with
5.21
-
(3)
gives the following chain of equivalences

L_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)_{x}\simeq\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(L_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F))(x)\simeq\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)(x)

6.11
is thus proved.
∎

Corollary 6.12
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
(X,Q)
be a conical refinement of
P
.
Assume that the strata of
(X,P)
and
(X,Q)
are locally weakly contractible.
Then the natural map

f\colon\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q)\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)

is a localization.

Proof.

We first reduce to the case where the stratification on
X
is trivial.
Let
W
be the collection of
f
-local equivalences in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q)
.
Since
f
is essentially surjective,
[
C
, 7.1.7, 7.1.11]
shows that it is enough to prove that the functor

f^{*}\colon\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q),\mathcal{S})

is fully faithful and its essential image consists of
W
-local objects.
Since by definition
f=\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(\mathrm{id}_{X})
,
6.8
shows that
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
makes the diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{S})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q),\mathcal{S})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{X,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}

commutative, while
5.17
shows that the vertical functors are equivalences.
Considering
\mathrm{Cons}_{Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
and
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
as full subcategories of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
, we see that
f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}
acts as the identity and it is therefore fully faithful.
Thus, the commutativity of the above diagram implies that the functor
f^{*}
is fully faithful as well.
Since
f
takes (by definition) arrows in
W
to equivalences, we see that
f^{*}
factors through the full subcategory of
W
-local objects.
Thus, we are left to check the essential surjectivity.
Let
F\colon\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q)\to\mathcal{S}
be a functor and assume that it is
W
-local.
In virtue of
5.17
, it is enough to prove that
\Psi_{X,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
belongs to
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
.
Denote by
\varphi\colon Q\to P
be the given morphism at the level of posets.
Let
a\in P
be a point and let
Q_{a}\coloneqq\varphi^{-1}(a)
.
Denote by
j_{a}\colon X_{Q_{a}}\to X
be the inclusion of the corresponding union of strata.
Applying
4.9
we see that the transformation

\psi_{j_{a}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon j_{a}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\big(\Psi_{X,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\big)\longrightarrow\Psi_{X_{Q_{a}},Q_{a}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(j_{a})^{\ast}(F)\big)

is an equivalence.
Thus, we can replace
X
by
X_{a}
.
Equivalently, we can assume from the very beginning that
P
is the trivial stratification (and therefore that
X
is locally weakly contractible).

Equipping
*
with the trivial stratification
S
, we can see the canonical map
\Gamma_{X}\colon X\to*
as a map of stratified spaces
(X,Q)\to(*,S)
.
Then
6.8
implies that
\psi_{\Gamma_{X}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
yields a canonical identification

\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\simeq\Psi_{X,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Gamma_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q)}^{\ast}\ .

Since
X
is locally weakly contractible,
[
HPT
, § 2.2]
shows that
\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}
has a left adjoint
\Gamma_{X,\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
.
The above identification implies

\Gamma_{X,\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\simeq\Gamma_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q),!}\circ\Phi_{X,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\ .

Using
[
HPT
, Corollary 3.5]
we find a canonical identification
\Pi_{\infty}(X)\simeq\Gamma_{X,\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(*))
.
Thus, the above identification yields:

\Pi_{\infty}(X)\simeq\Gamma_{X,\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\Gamma_{X}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(*)\simeq\colim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q)}\Gamma_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q)}^{\ast}(*)\simeq\mathrm{Env}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,Q))\ ,

whence the conclusion.
∎

We conclude this subsection with
6.13
showing that exodromy implies enhanced functorialities at the level of hyperconstructible hypersheaves:

Observation 6.13
.

Let
f\colon(X,P)\to(Y,Q)
be an exodromic morphism between conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata, and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category satisfying the assumption of
5.17
.
The functor
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}
has both a left adjoint given by the left Kan extension
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)_{!}
and a right adjoint given by the right Kan extension
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)_{\ast}
.
Thus, it follows that the functor

f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cons}_{Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})

also has a left and a right adjoint.
We set

f_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{cons}}\coloneqq\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)_{!}\circ\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{\ast}^{\mathrm{cons}}\coloneqq\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)_{\ast}\circ\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\ .

Since the functor
f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})
has a right adjoint given by
f_{\ast}
, and the inclusions of
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
and
\mathrm{Cons}_{Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})
have right adjoints
\mathrm{R}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\mathrm{R}_{Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
(see
5.21
), it follows from formal reasons that

(6.14)

f_{\ast}^{\mathrm{cons}}\simeq\mathrm{R}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f_{\ast}\ .

When
f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}
has itself a left adjoint
3
3

3

This is e.g. the case if
f
is the projection
S\times X\to S
for locally weakly contractible topological spaces, see
[
HPT
, § 2.2]
.

f_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
, it follows similarly that

f_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{cons}}\simeq\mathrm{L}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\ .

6.3.
Change of coefficients revisited

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
f:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
be a cocontinuous functor between presentable
\infty
-categories with right adjoint
g:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
.
Assume that the conditions of
5.17
are satisfied.
Let
p\in P
and let
i_{p}:X_{p}\to X
be the inclusion.
Then the following commutative diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{D})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\circ-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{p,\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{p,\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\circ-}

is verticaly left adjointable on
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})
.
That is, for every
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})
, the Beck-Chevalley transformation

i_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ g(F)\longrightarrow g\circ i_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The question is local on
X
.
Hence, we can suppose that
(X,P)
is of the form
Z\times C(Y)
where
Z
is a locally weakly contractible topological space and where
(Y,P_{>p})
is a stratified space.
Let
U
be an open subset of
Z
.
Then,
5.23
gives

(g\circ i_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))(U)=g(i_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)(U))\simeq g(F(U\times C(Y)))

On the other hand, we have

(i_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ g(F))(U)\simeq\colim_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}g(F(U\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y)))

We know from
5.22
that for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, the restriction morphism

F(U\times C(Y))\longrightarrow F(U\times C_{\varepsilon}(Y))

is an equivalence.
Hence, the above colimit is constant and we get

(i_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ g(F))(U)\simeq g(F(U\times C(Y)))

6.15
is thus proved.
∎

The following
6.16
contrasts with
2.29
.

Corollary 6.16
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
Let
f:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
be a cocontinuous functor between presentable
\infty
-categories with right adjoint
g:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
.
Assume that the conditions of
5.17
are satisfied.
Then, the following statements hold :

(1)

For every
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
, the functor
f\circ F:\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{D}
lies in
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})
.

(2)

For every
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})
, the functor
g\circ F:\mathrm{Open}(X)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathcal{E}
lies in
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
In particular, the adjunction
Eq.

2.28
induces an adjunction

f\circ-\colon\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\leftrightarrows\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})\colon g\circ-

(3)

The Exodromy equivalence induces an equivalence of adjunctions

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ-:\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D}):g\circ-}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ-:\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{D}):g\circ-\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{D}}}

Proof.

The first statement follows from
5.17
and
3.9
.
The statement
(2)
follows from
6.15
and
2.30
.
The statement
(3)
follows from
3.9
.
∎

6.4.
Monoidal structures

Assume now that our
\infty
-category of coefficients
\mathcal{E}
carries a presentably symmetric monoidal structure.
In other words, we fix
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L},\otimes})
.

Recollection 6.17
.

(1)

For every topological space
X
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
inherits a canonical symmetric monoidal structure, and every morphism
f\colon X\to Y
induces a symmetric monoidal functor

f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\ .

In particular, it follows that
\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
is stable under tensor product, and consequently that for every stratified space
(X,P)
,
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X,P)
acquires a symmetric monoidal structure.

(2)

Similarly,
\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E}\big)
inherits a symmetric monoidal structure, where the tensor product is computed objectwise.

Proposition 6.18
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}^{\otimes}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.
Then the functor

\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E}\big)\ ,

has a natural lax symmetric monoidal structure.
Under the assumptions of
5.17
,
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp},\mathcal{E}}
restricts to a symmetric monoidal functor on the full subcategory
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

Thanks to
3.8
, we immediately reduce to the case where
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{S}
.
In this case, the monoidal structure induced on both
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
and
\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{S}\big)
is the cartesian one.
Then
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
has a canonical lax monoidal structure given by
[
HA
, Proposition 2.4.1.7]
.
Since
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence, it commutes with products and hence Corollary 2.4.1.8 in
loc. cit.
guarantees that
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is strong monoidal.
The conclusion follows.
∎

As a consequence of our criterion for constructibility (see
5.22
), we obtain:

Corollary 6.19
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
If the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, then the internal hom
\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}_{X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
, computed inside
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
, belongs to
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

We apply
5.22
.
It is enough to prove that for every inclusion
i\colon U\subset V
for which the induced map
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i)\colon\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P)\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V,P)
is an equivalence, the restriction map

\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}_{X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})(V)\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}\mathrm{om}_{X}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})(U)

is an equivalence as well.
By definition, this map can be rewritten as

(6.20)

\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(V;\mathcal{E})}(\mathcal{F}|_{V},\mathcal{G}|_{V})\longrightarrow\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(U;\mathcal{E})}(\mathcal{F}|_{U},\mathcal{G}|_{U})\ .

5.17
allows to write the target of this morphism

\Hom_{\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U,P),\mathcal{E})}(\Phi_{U,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{F}|_{U}),\Phi_{U,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{G}|_{U}))\ ,

and similarly for the source.
Moreover, since the natural transformation

\phi^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{i}\colon\Phi_{U,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i)^{\ast}\circ\Phi_{V,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}

is an equivalence thanks to
6.8
, the morphism (
6.20
) becomes canonically identified with the morphism induced by
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i)^{\ast}

\Hom_{V}(\Phi_{V,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{F}|_{V}),\Phi_{V,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{G}|_{V}))\longrightarrow\Hom_{U}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i)^{\ast}\Phi_{V,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{F}|_{V}),\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i)^{\ast}\Phi_{V,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\mathcal{G}|_{V}))\ ,

where we wrote
\Hom_{V}
to denote the
\mathcal{E}
-enriched hom object computed in
\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V,P),\mathcal{E})
, and similarly for
\Hom_{U}
.
Since
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(i)^{\ast}
is an equivalence, so is the above morphism, whence the conclusion.
∎

6.5.
Compact generation

Using the extra functorialities obtained at the end of the previous subsection, we are now able to prove the following result, strengthening
6.4
:

Theorem 6.21
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a compactly generated presentable
\infty
-category with a single compact generator.
Assume that the poset
P
is finite and that for every
p\in P
, the set
\pi_{0}(X_{p})
is finite as well.
Then the
\infty
-category
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
has a single compact generator.

The proof relies on the following two simple observations:

Observation 6.22
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
x\in X
be a point and let
j_{x}\colon\{x\}\to X
be the natural inclusion.
The stratification on
\{x\}
induced by
P
is the trivial one.
In particular,
6.13
guarantees that the functor

j_{x}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}

admits a left adjoint
j_{x,\sharp}^{\mathrm{cons}}
which can be identified with the left Kan extension along the induced morphism
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(j_{x})\colon\Pi_{\infty}(\{x\})\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
.
Since
j_{x}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}
has also a right adjoint, it commutes with filtered colimits, and hence
j_{x,\sharp}^{\mathrm{cons}}
preserves
\kappa
-compact objects for every regular cardinal
\kappa
.

Observation 6.23
.

Let
X
be a locally weakly contractible topological space.
Assume that
X
is connected and let
x\in X
be a point.
Then the induced morphism
\Pi_{\infty}(\{x\})\to\Pi_{\infty}(X)
is an epimorphism in
\mathcal{S}
.
It follows that for every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
the restriction functor

\Pi_{\infty}(j_{x})^{\ast}:\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}(X),\mathcal{E}\big)\longrightarrow\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}(\{x\}),\mathcal{E}\big)\simeq\mathcal{E}

is conservative.
If furthermore
\mathcal{E}
satisfies the assumptions of
5.17
,
6.8
implies that the functor

j_{x}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}

is conservative.

Proof of
6.21
.

Since
\mathcal{E}
is compactly generated,
5.18
ensures that the hyperrestriction functors

\left\{j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{E})\right\}_{p\in P}

are jointly conservative.
Furthermore,
6.13
guarantees that they have left adjoints
j_{p,\sharp}^{\mathrm{cons}}
.
Therefore, their product

\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\prod_{p\in P}\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{E})\ ,

is conservative and admits a left adjoint.
Furthermore, it commutes with (filtered) colimits.
Thus,
[
AGV
, Lemma 2.8.3]
reduces us to check that the target has a single compact generator.
Since this properties are closed under finite products, it is enough to consider the case of a single stratum.

When the stratification on
X
is trivial, our assumption guarantees that the number of connected components of
X
is finite.
Hence, we are left to treat the case where
X
is connected.
Let then
x\in X
be a point and consider the functor

j_{x}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\ .

It follows from
6.23
that this functor is conservative, while
6.22
shows that it has a left adjoint.
Since it also commutes with filtered colimits, using once more
[
AGV
, Lemma 2.8.3]
we see that if
\{E_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is a family of compact generators for
\mathcal{E}
, then
\big\{j_{x,\sharp}^{\mathrm{cons}}(E_{i})\big\}_{i\in I}
is a family of compact generators for
\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
In particular, since
\mathcal{E}
has a single compact generator, then so does
\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
∎

For future reference, let us record the following more specific statement that can be deduced from the previous proof:

Corollary 6.24
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a compactly generated presentable
\infty
-category with compact generators
\{E_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in J}
.
Assume that the poset
P
is finite and that for every
p\in P
, the set
\pi_{0}(X_{p})
is finite as well.
Then

\left\{j_{x_{i},\sharp}^{\mathrm{cons}}(E_{\alpha})\right\}_{i\in I,\alpha\in J}

is a system of compact generators for
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

This result is already interesting in the case of the trivial stratification:

Example 6.25
.

Let
X
be a connected, locally weakly contractible topological space and let
x\in X
be a point.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category with a single compact generator
E\in\mathcal{E}
(e.g.
\mathcal{E}=\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
for some
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra
A
, and
E=A
).
Then
j_{x,\sharp}(A)\in\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
is a single compact generator.

As a consequence, we can express
\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
as the
\infty
-category of modules over the endomorphism ring of
j_{x,\sharp}(A)
.
It turns out that it is possible to compute quite explicitly such endomorphism ring:
4
4

4

We warmly thank Julian V. Holstein and Alexandru Oancea for discussions on this point.

Theorem 6.26
.

Let
X
be a connected and locally weakly contractible topological space.
Fix a point
x\in X
and consider the
x
-based loop space
\Omega_{x}(X)
.
Then for every
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra
A
, the endomorphism ring of
j_{x,\sharp}(A)
canonically coincides with the chain algebra
\mathrm{C}_{\ast}(\Omega_{x}(X);A)
.
In particular, there is an equivalence

\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{C}_{\ast}(\Omega_{x}(X);A)}\ .

Remark 6.27
.

This theorem recovers
[
Ha
, Theorem 26]
and
[
Hb
, Theorem 12]
.
Besides it generalizes it in several directions, as
A
is now allowed to be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra, and
X
is now only required to be locally weakly contractible rather than admitting a bounded locally finite good hypercover.
In particular, the above theorem holds even without any paracompactness assumption on
X
.

Proof of
6.26
.

The second half is a direct consequence of the first half and Lurie-Barr-Beck’s theorem
[
HA
, Theorem 4.7.3.5]
.
To compute the endomorphism ring of
j_{x,\sharp}(A)
, we first observe that there is the following natural equivalence:

\Hom_{\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})}\big(j_{x,\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(A),j_{x,\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(A)\big)\simeq j_{x}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}j_{x,\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(A)\ .

Observe now that the square

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}(\Omega_{x}(X))}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}(X)\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{x}}

is a pullback in
\mathcal{S}
, where we wrote
\Gamma
instead of
\Gamma_{\Omega_{x}(X)}
for brevity.
Unraveling the definitions, this implies that
\Pi_{\infty}(\Omega_{x}(X))
coincides with the comma category

\{x\}\times_{\Pi_{\infty}(X)}\Pi_{\infty}(X)_{j_{x}/}\ ,

so that combining
3.32
with the formula for the left Kan extension, we deduce that the Beck-Chevalley transformation

\Gamma_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Gamma^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow j_{x}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ j_{x,\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}

is an equivalence.
Thus, the endomorphism ring of
j_{x,\sharp}(A)
is canonically identified with

\Gamma_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\Gamma^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(A)\simeq\Gamma_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\Gamma^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(*)\otimes A\ .

By definition,
\Gamma_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\Gamma^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(*)\in\mathcal{S}
is the shape of the
\infty
-topos
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(\Omega_{x}(X))
.
Applying
[
HPT
, Corollary 3.5]
, we see that this shape is simply identified with the homotopy type
\Pi_{\infty}(\Omega_{x}(X))
.
Thus, the endomorphism ring of
j_{x,\sharp}(A)
is identified with

\Gamma_{\sharp}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\Gamma^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(A)\simeq\Pi_{\infty}(\Omega_{x}(X))\otimes A\simeq\mathrm{C}_{\ast}(\Omega_{x}(X);A)\ ,

where the last equivalence holds by definition of singular chains.
∎

Remark 6.28
.

It is possible to obtain a similar description in the stratified case, in line with
[
HL
, §6]
.
It should be possible to obtain an explicit description of the endomorphism ring of the single compact generator provided by
6.21
akin to the one of
6.26
, at least in the setting of conically smooth stratified spaces.
Indeed, any such description should see the chain algebras on single strata, but at the same time, it should also have a contribution from the links of the stratification.
We will come back to this subject in a later work.

6.6.
Exodromy and stalkwise compactness

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
The construction of
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
relies a priori on the existence of limits and colimits of diagrams that are typically not finite.
However, the “regularity” of conical charts paired with the homotopy-invariance property of hyperconstructible hypersheaves
[
HPT
, Theorem 0.4]
