actually implies that all infinite colimits that are involved in the construction of the exodromy adjunction can be ignored.
To give a proper formulation of this idea, let us first introduce the following notation:

Notation 6.29
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Let
\kappa
be a regular cardinal.
We denote by
\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\kappa}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
spanned by
\mathcal{E}
-valued hyperconstructible hypersheaves
F
whose stalks are
\kappa
-compact objects of
\mathcal{E}
.
When the stratification
P
is trivial, we denote this
\infty
-category by
\mathrm{LC}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

Warning 6.30
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\kappa}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
does not coincide neither with
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E}^{\kappa})
nor with
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})^{\kappa}
.
We offer two counterexamples:

(1)

Take
X\coloneqq\coprod_{\mathbb{N}}*
to be an infinite disjoint union of points equipped with the trivial stratification.
Take also
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{S}
and
\kappa=\omega
.
Fix
K\in\mathcal{S}^{\kappa}
and let
F\coloneqq\Gamma_{X}^{\ast}(K)
be the hyperconstant hypersheaf associated to
K
.
Then
F\in\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\kappa}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
, but
F(X)\simeq\prod_{\mathbb{N}}K
, which does not belong to
\mathcal{S}^{\omega}
unless
K
is contractible.
In particular,
F
does not belong to
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{S}^{\omega})
.

(2)

Take
X=S^{1}
,
\mathcal{E}=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
\kappa=\omega
.
Once again, equip
X
with the trivial stratification.
Then, we have canonical identifications

\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(S^{1};\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{C}})\simeq\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}(S^{1}),\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{C}})\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}})\ .

Therefore,

\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(S^{1};\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{C}})^{\omega}\simeq\mathrm{Perf}(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}})\ .

In particular, the structure sheaf of
\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}
corresponds to the functor
F\colon S^{1}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{C}}
selecting
\mathbb{C}[T,T^{-1}]
with endomorphism given by multiplication by
T
.
Then
6.10
implies that the stalk of
\Psi_{S^{1}}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
at any point of
S^{1}
coincides with
\mathbb{C}[T,T^{-1}]
, which is not compact as an object in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Remark 6.31
.

In the second example above, it follows from
[
BZNP
]
that
\mathrm{LC}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(S^{1};\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{C}})
corresponds to the full subcategory of
\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}})
spanned by perfect complexes
with proper support
.

Proposition 6.32
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Let
\kappa
be a regular cardinal.
If the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, then the exodromy equivalence
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\dashv\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
restricts to an equivalence

\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E}^{\kappa})\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\kappa}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\colon\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\ .

Proof.

It is enough to prove that both
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
respect these two full subcategories.
Let first
F\in\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\kappa}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
Then we have to check that the functor
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
takes values in
\mathcal{E}^{\kappa}
.
To see this, let
x\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
.
Then
6.10
provides a canonical identification

\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)(x)\simeq F_{x}

so that
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
belongs to
\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E}^{\kappa})
.

Let now
G\colon\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)\to\mathcal{E}^{\kappa}
be a functor.
Let
x\in X
.
Then
6.10
and
5.17
provide the following canonical identifications:

\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(G)_{x}\simeq\big(\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(G)\big)(x)\simeq G(x)\ .

Thus, the stalks of
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(G)
belong to
\mathcal{E}^{\kappa}
.
In other words,
\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(G)
belongs to
\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\kappa}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
∎

6.7.
Constructibility and pushforward. A general criterion

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be an exodromic morphism of conically stratified spaces.
Then the natural transformation
\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
makes the square

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(Y,Q),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}

commutative.
In particular, there is an associated Beck-Chevalley transformation

\chi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)_{\ast}\longrightarrow f_{\ast}\circ f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)_{\ast}\xrightarrow{\psi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}}f_{\ast}\circ\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}\circ\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)_{\ast}\longrightarrow f_{\ast}\circ\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\ .

The identification (
6.14
) has the following immediate consequence:

Lemma 6.33
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be an exodromic morphism of conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category and assume that the conditions of
5.17
are satisfied.
Then for every
F\colon\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(Y,Q)\to\mathcal{E}
, the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

the hypersheaf
f_{\ast}\big(\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\big)\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
is hyperconstructible on
(X,P)
;

(2)

the transformation
\chi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\colon\Psi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)_{\ast}(F)\big)\to f_{\ast}\big(\Psi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\big)
is an equivalence.

The following observation gives a convenient sufficient condition to check whether pushforward along a morphism
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
preserves hyperconstructible hypersheaves:

Lemma 6.34
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a strongly exodromic morphism of conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category and assume that the conditions of
5.17
are satisfied.
Then
f_{\ast}
preserves hyperconstructible hypersheaves.

Proof.

By assumption, the natural transformation
\phi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
makes the diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(Y,Q),\mathcal{E})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(f)^{\ast}}

commutative.
Passing to right adjoints, we deduce that the natural transformation
\chi_{f}^{\mathrm{hyp}}
is an equivalence.
Then
6.34
follows from
4.10
.
∎

6.8.
Constructibility and pushforward along immersions

Proposition 6.35
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Let
S\subseteq P
be a locally closed subset of
P
and let
i_{S}\colon X_{S}\to X
be the inclusion of the corresponding union of strata.
If the assumption of
5.17
are satisfied, then

i_{S,\ast}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})

takes hyperconstructible hypersheaves on
(X_{S},S)
to hyperconstructible hypersheaves on
(X,P)
.

Proof.

Combine Corollaries
5.8
and
5.15
with
6.34
.
∎

6.35
has the following two immediate corollaries below which are true for hyperconstructible hypersheaves since they are already true for hypersheaves.

Corollary 6.36
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
S\subseteq P
be a locally closed subset.
Let
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{D}
be presentable
\infty
-categories and let
f\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
be a cocontinuous functor.
If the assumption of
5.17
are satisfied, then the diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{S}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{S}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{D})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{S}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{S}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}

is horizontally right adjointable.
Than is, the Beck-Chevalley transformation

f\circ i_{S,\ast}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow i_{S,\ast}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f

is an equivalence.

For the notion of recollement of
\infty
-categories, let us refer to
[
HA
, A.8.1]
.

Corollary 6.37
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Let
S\subseteq P
be a closed subset and let
i_{S}\colon X_{S}\to X
be the inclusion.
Put
U:=P\setminus S
and let
i_{U}\colon X_{U}\to X
be the inclusion.
If the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, then the fully-faithful functors

i_{S,\ast}\colon\mathrm{Cons}_{S}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longleftarrow\mathrm{Cons}_{U}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{U};\mathcal{E})\colon i_{U,\ast}

exhibits
\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
as a recollement of
\mathrm{Cons}_{S}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})
and
\mathrm{Cons}_{U}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{U};\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

6.37
follows immediately from the fact that the fully-faithful functors

i_{S,\ast}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longleftarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{U};\mathcal{E})\colon i_{U,\ast}

exhibits
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
as a recollement of
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})
and
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{U};\mathcal{E})
.
∎

As for the hyperrestriction of a hyperconstructible hypersheaf to a stratum computed in
5.23
, one can compute the fibre of
i_{S,\ast}
at a point
x
explicitly in terms of a conical chart containing
x
.
This is the following

Lemma 6.38
.

Let
Z
be a weakly contractible locally weakly contractible topological space and let
(Y,Q)
be a stratified space such that
\mathrm{Exit}(Y,Q)
is an
\infty
-category and
(X,P)\coloneqq(Z\times C(Y),Q^{\vartriangleleft})
is conically stratified.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Let
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
and let
x\in Z
.
If the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, there is a canonical identification

(i_{Q,\ast}F)_{x}\simeq\lim_{\mathrm{Exit}(Y,Q)}\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)|_{\mathrm{Exit}(Y,Q)}

where in the right-hand side, the restriction is performed along the equivalence
\mathrm{Exit}(Z\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}\times Y,Q)\to\mathrm{Exit}(Y,Q)
induced by the canonical projection.

Proof.

Write
\mathcal{B}_{x}
for the collection of weakly contractible open neighborhoods of
x
inside
Z
.
Then

i_{Q,\ast}(F)_{x}\simeq\colim_{U\in\mathcal{B}_{x}}\colim_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}F(U\times(0,\varepsilon)\times Y)\ .

From
5.17
and
Eq.

3.13
, we deduce

i_{Q,\ast}(F)_{x}\simeq\colim_{U\in\mathcal{B}_{x}}\colim_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}\lim_{\mathrm{Exit}(U\times(0,\epsilon)\times Y,Q)}\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\ .

Since the
U\in\mathcal{B}_{x}
are weakly contractible, we have further

\mathrm{Exit}(U\times(0,\epsilon)\times Y,Q)\simeq\mathrm{Exit}(U,\ast)\times\mathrm{Exit}((0,\epsilon),\ast)\times\mathrm{Exit}(Y,Q)\simeq\mathrm{Exit}(Y,Q)\ .

Hence, the above colimits are constant and
6.38
thus follows.
∎

In view of the construction of moduli carried out in
Section

7
, we need to understand the interaction of push-forward with proper hyperconstructible hypersheaves.
To this end, additional finiteness assumptions on
(X,P)
will be needed.
They will be justified by the following general

Lemma 6.39
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\omega}
be a compact object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Assume that filtered colimits are left exact in
\mathcal{E}
.
Then the functor

\Gamma_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\colon\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}

commutes with filtered colimits.
When
\mathcal{E}
is stable, both the left adjoint
\Gamma_{\mathcal{C},!}
and the right adjoint
\Gamma_{\mathcal{C},\ast}
to
\Gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}
restrict to functors

\Gamma_{\mathcal{C},!},\ \Gamma_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\colon\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E}^{\omega})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\omega}\ .

Proof.

The functor
\Gamma_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}
takes
\mathcal{E}^{\omega}
in
\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E}^{\omega})
by definition.
It is therefore enough to prove that
\Gamma_{\mathcal{C},\ast}
commutes with filtered colimits and that in the stable case it restricts it takes
\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E}^{\omega})
to
\mathcal{E}^{\omega}
. Let
\mathcal{T}_{0}(\mathcal{E})
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
spanned by those
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{C}
for which the functor
\Gamma_{\mathcal{C},\ast}
commutes with filtered colimits.
Let
\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{E})
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
spanned by those
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{C}
for which the functors
\Gamma_{\mathcal{C},!}
and
\Gamma_{\mathcal{C},\ast}
take
\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E}^{\omega})
to
\mathcal{E}^{\omega}
.
Inspection reveals that
\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{E})
is closed under finite colimits, retractions and contain all finite
1
-categories (that is,
1
-categories having a finite number of objects and of morphisms).
Since
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
is compactly generated by the
1
-categories
\Delta^{n}
, the conclusion follows.
∎

Definition 6.40
.

We say that a stratified space
(X,P)
is
categorically compact
if
\mathrm{Exit}(X,P)
is a compact object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.

Definition 6.41
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space.
We say that
(X,P)
is
locally categorically compact
if for every
p\in P
and every
x\in X_{p}
, there exists a conical chart of the form
Z\times C(Y)
containing
x
such that
(Y,P_{>p})
is categorically compact and
Z
is weakly contractible and locally weakly contractible.

Proposition 6.42
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Assume that
(X,P)
is locally categorically compact and that
P
is finite.
Let
S\subseteq P
be a locally closed subset and let
\mathcal{E}
be a stable presentable
\infty
-category.
Then the functor

i_{S,\ast}\colon\mathrm{Cons}_{S}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})

commutes with filtered colimits and it restricts to a functor

i_{S,\ast}\colon\mathrm{Cons}_{S,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\ .

Proof.

When
S
is closed inside
P
, the functor
i_{S,\ast}
coincides with the extension by zero and
6.42
is trivial in that case.
It is then enough to consider the case where
S
is open inside
P
.
We proceed by induction on
\mathrm{depth}(P)
.
When
\mathrm{depth}(P)=0
, the stratification is trivial and therefore we have either
S=\emptyset
or
S=P
.
In both cases, the statement is obvious.

Assume now that
\mathrm{depth}(X)>0
.
Since
P
is finite, the subset
M\subseteq P
of its minimal elements is finite as well.
Writing
M=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}\}
, we see that
\{X_{\geqslant p_{i}}\}_{i=1,\ldots,n}
is an open cover of
X
.
Since both the compacity of stalks and the formation of filtered colimits are local statements on
X
, we can assume that
P
has a minimum
p
.
Using the inductive hypothesis, we further reduce to the case where
S=P\smallsetminus M
.

Let now
F\in\mathrm{Cons}_{S,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})
.
We have to prove that the stalks of
i_{S,\ast}(F)
belong to
\mathcal{E}^{\omega}
.
Since
i_{S,\ast}
is fully faithful, we only have to prove this statement for the stalks at a point
x\in X_{p}
.
Choose a conical chart of the form
Z\times C(Y)
, where
(Y,S)
is a categorically compact stratified space and where
Z
is weakly contractible and locally weakly contractible.
From
6.38
, we have a canonical identification

(i_{S,\ast}F)_{x}\simeq\lim_{\mathrm{Exit}(Y,S)}\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)|_{\mathrm{Exit}(Y,S)}

Since
\mathrm{Exit}(Y,S)
is a compact object of
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
and since the functor
\Phi_{X,P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
takes values in
\mathcal{E}^{\omega}
,
6.42
thus follows from
6.39
.
The same method also guarantees that
i_{S,\ast}
commutes with filtered colimits.
∎

6.37
immediately gives the following

Corollary 6.43
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Let
S\subseteq P
be a closed subset and let
i_{S}\colon X_{S}\to X
be the inclusion.
Put
U:=P\setminus S
and let
i_{U}\colon X_{U}\to X
be the inclusion.
If the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied, then the fully-faithful functors

i_{S,\ast}\colon\mathrm{Cons}_{S,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longleftarrow\mathrm{Cons}_{U,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{U};\mathcal{E})\colon i_{U,\ast}

exhibits
\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
as a recollement of
\mathrm{Cons}_{S,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})
and
\mathrm{Cons}_{U,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{U};\mathcal{E})
.

6.36
and
6.42
immediately gives the following

Corollary 6.44
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Assume that
(X,P)
is locally categorically compact and that
P
is finite.
Let
S\subseteq P
be a locally closed subset and let
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{D}
stable presentable
\infty
-categories.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{D}
be a cocontinuous functor preserving compact objects.
Then, the diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{S,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{P,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{D})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cons}_{S,\omega}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{S};\mathcal{D})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{S}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{S}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}}

is horizontally right adjointable.
Than is, the Beck-Chevalley transformation

f\circ i_{S,\ast}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow i_{S,\ast}^{\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f

is an equivalence.

6.9.
Constructibility, pushforward and weakly stratified bundles

We now present a second result concerning proper pushforward.
Lurie proved proper non abelian base change for sheaves on locally compact Hausdorff spaces.
See
[
HTT
, Corollary 7.3.1.18]
.
Note however that it is not clear that proper base change holds for hypersheaves.
We are going to see that under some additional assumptions on the stratifications involved, proper base change holds for
hyperconstructible
hypersheaves.

Observation 6.45
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a proper morphism between topological spaces.
Following
[
HTT
, Definition 7.3.1.14]
, this means for us that
f
is universally closed.
Let
C\subseteq X
be a locally closed subset of
X
.
Write
\mathcal{B}_{C}
for the collection of open neighborhoods of
C
inside
X
.
Then
\{f^{-1}(U)\}_{U\in\mathcal{B}_{C}}
is a fundamental system of open neighborhoods for the inverse image
f^{-1}(C)
.
Indeed, since
f
is universally closed we can localize on
X
and therefore assume that
C
is closed.
Let now
V
be an open neighborhood of
f^{-1}(C)
inside
Y
.
Since
f
is closed,
f(Y\smallsetminus V)
is a closed subset of
X
.
Furthermore
f(Y\smallsetminus V)\cap C=\emptyset
, so
U\coloneqq X\smallsetminus f(Y\smallsetminus V)
is an open neighborhood of
C
inside
X
.
We now observe that if
y\in f^{-1}(U)
, then
f(y)\notin f(Y\smallsetminus V)
, which in turn implies that
y\in V
.
Therefore,
f^{-1}(U)\subseteq V
.

Before proving the sought after proper base change for hyperconstructible hypersheaves, we need a strengthening of the notion of excellency introduced in
2.18
.

Definition 6.46
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space and let
S\subseteq P
be a subset.
We say that
(X,P)
is
hereditary excellent at
S
if for every open subset
U\subseteq X
, the stratified space
(U,P)
is excellent at
S
.

Proposition 6.47
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata and let
S\subseteq P
be a locally closed subset.
Let
\varphi\colon Q\to P
be the underlying morphism of posets and set
R\coloneqq\varphi^{-1}(S)
.
Consider the induced commutative square

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y_{R},R)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y,Q)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{S},S)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,P)\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

Assume that:

(1)

the underlying morphism
f\colon Y\to X
is proper;

(2)

(Y,Q)
is hereditary excellent at
R
.

Then for every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
satisfying the assumptions of
5.17
and every
F\in\mathrm{Cons}_{Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})
the canonical map

i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(f_{\ast}(F))\longrightarrow g_{\ast}(j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The statement is local on
X
.
We can therefore suppose that
S
is a closed downwards subset of
P
.
Since source and target are hypersheaves, it is enough to prove that for every open subset
U
of
X_{S}
the induced morphism

i^{-1}(f_{\ast}(F))(U)\longrightarrow g_{\ast}(j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))(U)

is an equivalence.
Let
\mathcal{B}_{U}
for the collection of open neighborhoods of
U
inside
X
.
Since
f
is proper and
U
is locally closed inside
X
,
6.45
shows that
\{f^{-1}(V)\}_{V\in\mathcal{B}_{U}}
is a fundamental system of open neighborhoods of
g^{-1}(U)=f^{-1}(U)
inside
Y
.
Since
(Y,Q)
is hereditary excellent at
R
, the collection
\mathcal{B}_{g^{-1}(U)}^{\mathrm{exc}}
of excellent open neighborhoods of
g^{-1}(U)
inside
Y
is a fundamental system of open neighborhoods for
g^{-1}(U)
.
Thus,
5.17
and
3.3
provide the following chain of natural equivalences:

i^{-1}(f_{\ast}(F))(U)\simeq\colim_{V\in\mathcal{B}_{U}}F(f^{-1}(V))\simeq\colim_{W\in\mathcal{B}_{g^{-1}(U)}^{\mathrm{exc}}}F(W)\simeq\colim_{W\in\mathcal{B}_{g^{-1}(U)}^{\mathrm{exc}}}\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(W,Q)}\Phi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\ .

Since each
W\in\mathcal{B}_{g^{-1}(U)}^{\mathrm{exc}}
is excellent at
R
, the functor
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(g^{-1}(U),R)\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(W,Q)
is final.
Therefore the colimit on the right is constant, and we deduce

i^{-1}(f_{\ast}(F))(U)\simeq\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(g^{-1}(U),R)}\Phi_{Y,Q}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)|_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(Y_{R},R)}

Since
j:Y_{R}\to Y
is exodromic, we deduce

i^{-1}(f_{\ast}(F))(U)\simeq\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(g^{-1}(U),R)}\Phi_{Y_{R},R}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))\simeq j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)(g^{-1}(U))\simeq g_{\ast}(j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))(U)\ .

The conclusion follows.
∎

Remark 6.48
.

In the above proof, properness is only used in the form of
6.45
.
It would therefore be enough to ask that for every
x\in X
the collection
\{f^{-1}(V)\}_{V\in\mathcal{B}_{x}}
form a fundamental system of open neighborhood for the fiber
f^{-1}(x)
inside
Y
(where
\mathcal{B}_{x}
denotes the collection of open neighborhoods of
x
inside
X
).

Definition 6.49
.

We say that a morphism between conically stratified spaces
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
is a
weak stratified bundle
if for every
p\in P
every point
x\in X_{p}
admits an open neighborhood
U
in
X_{p}
such that there exists a conically stratified space
(W,R)
and an isomorphism of stratified spaces

(f^{-1}(U),Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}(U\times W,R)

over
U
.

Proposition 6.50
.

Let
f\colon(Y,Q)\to(X,P)
be a morphism of conically stratified spaces with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Assume that:

(1)

the underlying morphism
f\colon Y\to X
is proper;

(2)

f
is a weak stratified bundle whose fibres have locally weakly contractible strata;

(3)

(Y,Q)
is hereditary excellent at every locally closed subset of
Q
;

(4)

the assumptions of
5.17
are satisfied.

Then
f_{\ast}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
preserves hyperconstructible hypersheaves and for every
F\in\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})
and every
x\in X
there is a canonical equivalence

f_{\ast}(F)_{x}\simeq\Gamma(Y_{x},j_{x}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))\ ,

where
j_{x}\colon Y_{x}\hookrightarrow Y
denotes the inclusion of the fiber.

Proof.

Using
6.47
, we immediately reduce to the case where
P
is trivial.
Since both statements are local on the target and since
f
is a weak stratified bundle, we can assume that
(Y,Q)\simeq(X\times W,R)
for some conically stratified space
(W,R)
and that
f
coincides with the canonical projection to
X
.
Let
F\in\mathrm{Cons}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Y;\mathcal{E})
.
We first show that
f_{\ast}(F)
is locally hyperconstant on
X
. To do this,
[
HPT
, Proposition 3.1]
ensures that it is enough to prove that for every inclusion
U\subseteq V
of weakly contractible open subsets in
X
, the restriction map

f_{\ast}(F)(V)\longrightarrow f_{\ast}(F)(U)

is an equivalence.
Since
F
is hyperconstructible,
3.21
and
5.17
allow to rewrite this map as

\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V\times W,R)}\Phi_{X\times W,R}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\longrightarrow\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U\times W,R)}\Phi_{X\times W,R}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\ .

Since
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}
commutes with finite products, we have

\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(V\times W,R)\simeq\Pi_{\infty}(V)\times\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(W,R),\qquad\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(U\times W,R)\simeq\Pi_{\infty}(U)\times\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(W,R)\ .

Since the map
\Pi_{\infty}(U)\to\Pi_{\infty}(V)
is an equivalence, the conclusion follows.

Let now
x\in X
.
To compute
f_{\ast}(F)_{x}
, we can further suppose that
X
is weakly contractible.
In that case, the local hyperconstancy of
f_{\ast}(F)
combined with
[
HPT
, Proposition 3.1]
ensures that the canonical map

f_{\ast}(F)(X)\longrightarrow f_{\ast}(F)_{x}

is an equivalence.
On the other hand,
3.21
combined with the fact that the morphism
Y_{x}=\{x\}\times W\to Y
is exodromic gives a chain of equivalences

\displaystyle f_{\ast}(F)(X)

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X\times W,R)}\Phi_{X\times W,R}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\simeq\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(\{x\}\times W,R)}\Phi_{X\times W,R}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(F)

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(Y_{x},R)}\Phi_{Y_{x},R}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(j_{x}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))\simeq\Gamma(Y_{x},j_{x}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))

The proof of
6.50
is thus complete.
∎

7.
Moduli spaces

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata and let
R
be an excellent commutative ring.
Consider the derived prestack

\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)\colon\mathrm{dAff}_{R}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}

defined by sending a derived affine scheme
\Spec(A)
over
R
to the maximal
\infty
-groupoid contained in

\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\simeq\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathrm{Perf}(A)\big)\ ,

where the equivalence is the one provided by
6.32
.
Observe that the right hand side commutes with limits in
\mathrm{Perf}(A)
.
In particular we obtain:

Lemma 7.1
.

The derived prestack
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
satisfies faithfully flat hyperdescent.
In particular, it is a derived stack.

Proof.

Combine
[
SAG
, Corollary D.6.3.3 and Proposition 2.8.4.2-(10)]
.
∎

Our goal is to prove that when
(X,P)
satisfies certain mild finiteness conditions,
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is locally geometric.

7.1.
Finiteness conditions on stratified homotopy types

Definition 7.2
.

For stratified space
(X,P)
, we say that :

(1)

(X,P)
is
categorically compact
if
\mathrm{Exit}(X,P)
is a compact object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
;

If furthermore
(X,P)
is conically stratified, we say that:

(2)

(X,P)
is
locally categorically compact
if for every
p\in P
and every
x\in X_{p}
, there exists a conical chart of the form
Z\times C(Y)
containing
x
such that
(Y,P_{>p})
is categorically compact and
Z
is weakly contractible and locally weakly contractible ;

(3)

(X,P)
is
of finite stratified type
if the poset
P
is finite and for every
p\in P
, the homotopy type
\Pi_{\infty}(X_{p})
of the associated stratum is a compact object in
\mathcal{S}
.

When the stratification is trivial, there is an abundance of examples:

Example 7.3
.

(1)

Assume that
X
is Stein complex (smooth) manifold, equipped with a trivial stratification.
Then classical results of Remmert
[
R
]
, Bishop
[
B
]
and Narasimhan
[
N
]
show that
X
can be realized as a closed subvariety of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
for
n\gg 0
.
In particular, Andreotti-Frankel’s theorem
[
AF
]
shows that
X
has the homotopy type of a finite CW complex, and in particular
\Pi_{\infty}(X)
is compact.

(2)

Assume now that
X
is a smooth algebraic variety.
If
X
is affine, then its analytification
X^{\mathrm{an}}
is smooth and Stein and therefore
\Pi_{\infty}(X^{\mathrm{an}})
is compact by the previous point.
In general,
X
admits a finite cover by affine smooth open subvarieties, whose intersections are again smooth and affine.
This immediately implies that
\Pi_{\infty}(X^{\mathrm{an}})
can be realized as a finite colimit of compact objects in
\mathcal{S}
, and henceforth that
\Pi_{\infty}(X^{\mathrm{an}})
is compact itself.

(3)

Assume that
X
is a
compact
topological manifold.
Then the work of Kirby and Siebenmann
[
KS
, Theorem III]
implies that
X
has the homotopy type of a
finite
CW complex (although
X
might not be triangulable itself).
In particular,
\Pi_{\infty}(X)
is a compact object in
\mathcal{S}
.

Another big class of examples comes from the theory of conically smooth stratified spaces.

Recollection 7.4
.

We refer the reader to
[
AFTb
, § 3.2]
for the notion of conically smooth stratified space.
Although their definition is long and involved, it is an extremely convenient setting to work in, as it enjoys the following properties:

(1)

every stratum
X_{p}
is a smooth manifold;

(2)

for every
p\in P
, every point
x\in X_{p}
has a fundamental system of open neighborhoods of the form
U\times C(Z)
, where
U
is a weakly contractible (and in fact homeomorphic to
\mathbb{R}^{i}
for some
i\geqslant 0
) open neighborhood of
x
in
X_{p}
and
Z
is a
compact
conically smooth stratified space over
P_{>p}
;

(3)

if the stratification on
(X,P)
is Whitney, then it is conically smooth, as recently shown by Nocera and Volpe
[
NV
, Theorem 2.7]
.

In other words, properties (1) and (2) enable inductive arguments on the depth of the stratification, while property (3) guarantees that every reasonable example considered in practice falls in this class.

Also, in
[
AFTb
, Definition 8.3.6]
the authors introduced the notion of
finitary
conically smooth stratified space, that intuitively corresponds to those conically smooth stratified spaces that admit a finite handlebody decomposition, such as the one provided by Morse theory.
Observe that
[
AFTb
, Theorem 8.3.10-(2)]
implies that every conically smooth stratified space
(X,P)
whose underlying topological space is compact is automatically finitary.

Having recalled these facts concerning conically smooth stratified spaces, we can state the following recent theorem of Volpe:

Theorem 7.5

(Volpe,
[
V
, Proposition 2.12 & Corollary 2.13]
)
.

Let
(X,P)
be a finitary conically smooth stratified space.
Then
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
is categorically equivalent to a simplicial set having only a finite number of non-degenerate simplexes. Hence,
(X,P)
is categorically compact.

Corollary 7.6
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically smooth stratified space.
Then
(X,P)
is locally categorically compact.

Proof.

Since
(X,P)
is a finitary conically smooth stratified space, for every
p\in P
and every
x\in X_{p}
there exists an open neighborhood of
x
of the form
U\times C(Z)
, where
U
is weakly contractible locally weakly contractible and
(Z,P_{>p})
is a
compact
conically smooth stratified space.
In particular,
[
AFTb
, Theorem 8.3.10-(2)]
implies that
(Z,P_{>p})
is finitary.
Then
7.5
implies that
(Z,P_{>p})
is categorically compact.
∎

When
(X,P)
is categorically compact, it is particularly easy to prove that
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is a locally geometric derived stack:

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
(X,P)
be a categorically compact conically stratified space.
Then the derived stack
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is locally geometric, and the tangent complex at a point
x\colon\Spec(A)\to\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
classifying a constructible sheaf
F\in\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
is given by

x^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)}\simeq\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})}(F,F)[1]\ ,

where the right hand side denotes the
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
-enriched
\Hom
of
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
.

Proof.

By assumption,
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
is a compact object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
Since
(-)^{\mathrm{op}}\colon\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
is a self-equivalence, we deduce that
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}}
is compact as well.
Moreover, every self map in
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}}
is automatically an equivalence, which in turn implies that
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
is idempotent complete.
In particular,

\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\mathrm{PSh}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}}\big)^{\omega}\simeq\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathcal{S}\big)^{\omega}\ .

Now, recall from
[
HTT
, Proposition 5.5.7.11 & Corollary 4.4.5.21]
that
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\mathsf{rex},\mathsf{idem}}
is closed under filtered colimits in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
It follows that
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}}
is compact in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\mathsf{rex},\mathsf{idem}}\simeq\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}
.
Thus,

\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\mathrm{PSh}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}}\big)\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\simeq\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})\ .

is automatically a compact object in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{R}
, and hence it is smooth and proper in the sense of
[
SAG
, §11]
.
In particular, its moduli of objects
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
in the sense of
[
TV
]
is well defined.
Unraveling the definitions, we see that the functor of points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
sends
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}_{R}
to

\displaystyle\Fun_{R}\big((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Perf}(A)\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\Fun_{R}\big((\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes\mathrm{Perf}(R))^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Perf}(A)\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\Fun_{R}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes\mathrm{Perf}(R),\mathrm{Perf}(A)^{\mathrm{op}}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\simeq\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Perf}(A)^{\mathrm{op}}\big)^{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\simeq\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathrm{Perf}(A)\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathrm{Perf}(A))\ .

Therefore, the conclusion follows from
[
TV
, Theorem 3.6 & Corollary 3.17]
.
∎

We can now state the main theorem of this section:

Theorem 7.8
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Assume that:

(1)

(X,P)
is of finite stratified type;

(2)

(X,P)
is locally categorically compact.

Then
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is a locally geometric derived stack, locally of finite presentation.

Remark 7.9
.

(1)

We do not know whether the assumptions of
7.8
imply that
(X,P)
is categorically compact.
By contrast with
7.7
, we are going to apply Lurie’s representability theorem to prove
7.8
.

(2)

7.8
covers
a priori
a larger class of stratified spaces
(X,P)
for which
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is a locally geometric derived stack than the one provided combining
7.5
and
7.7
.
Indeed, we do not know whether every conically smooth stratified space
(X,P)
which is also of finite stratified type is automatically finitary.
Nevertheless, we formulate the following conjecture, that would extend Volpe’s theorem:

Conjecture 7.10
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically smooth stratified space.
If
(X,P)
is of finite stratified type, then
(X,P)
is also categorically compact.

We do not know how to prove this conjecture at the moment.
However we obtain a very close result below (see
7.11
).

7.2.
Local finite presentation

Recall that if
\{A_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
is a filtered diagram in
\mathrm{CAlg}_{R}
with colimit
A
, the canonical map

\colim_{\alpha\in I}\mathrm{Perf}(A_{\alpha})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Perf}(A)

is an equivalence in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
as shown in
[
SAG
, Corollary 4.5.1.8]
.
Therefore, when
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
is compact in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
, the functor
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is automatically locally of finite type.
More generally, we have the following

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space satisfying the assumptions of
7.8
.
Then the functor

\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),(-)^{\omega}\big)\colon\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{\mathrm{st}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}

commutes with filtered colimits.
Hence
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is locally of finite presentation.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the cardinality
n
of
P
.
When
n=1
, the stratification is trivial and therefore
\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)=\Pi_{\infty}(X)
, which is compact in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
by assumption.
Hence, this case follows.
If
n>1
, take
p\in P
a minimal element and consider
S\coloneqq P\smallsetminus\{p\}
.
Then both
P_{\leqslant p}
and
S
have cardinality strictly less than
n
.
Consider a filtered diagram
I\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{\mathrm{st}}
, noted
\alpha\mapsto\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}
and let
\mathcal{E}
be its colimit computed in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{\mathrm{st}}
.
Consider the following diagram:

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim_{\alpha}\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{S,\omega}(X_{S};\mathcal{E}_{\alpha})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim_{\alpha}\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathcal{E}_{\alpha})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\colim_{\alpha}\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{p,\omega}(X_{p};\mathcal{E}_{\alpha})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{S,\omega}(X_{S};\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{p,\omega}(X_{p};\mathcal{E})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{S,\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{p,\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{S,\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{p,\ast}}

From
6.44
, each square is commutative.
The external vertical arrows are equivalences by inductive hypothesis.
The horizontal functors exhibit the middle terms as recollements of the external ones as a result of
6.43
.
Therefore, the middle vertical arrow is an equivalence as a result of
[
HA
, Proposition A.8.14]
.
We then conclude using the fact that the interior groupoïd functor commutes with filtered colimits.
∎

7.3.
Cohesiveness, nilcompleteness and integrability

For a review of definitions, let us refer to
[
SAG
, 17.3.7]

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Then the derived stack
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is infinitesimally cohesive, nilcomplete and satisfies integrability.

Proof.

These statements follow from the analogous ones for
\mathrm{Perf}
, the description of the category
\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
as
\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathrm{Perf}(A)\big)
and the commutation of the interior groupoid functor with limits.
Specifically, infinitesimally cohesiveness follows from
[
SAG
, Theorem 16.2.0.1 and Proposition 16.2.3.1]
. Nilcompleteness follows from
[
SAG
, Propositions 2.7.3.2 and 19.2.1.5]
.
Integrability follows from
[
SAG
, Remark 8.5.0.5 and Theorem 8.5.0.3]
.
∎

7.4.
Cotangent complex

The next important step to verify the assumptions of Artin-Lurie’s representability theorem is to check the existence of the cotangent complex for
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
.

Notation 7.13
.

In proving the existence of the cotangent complex of
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
, we will crucially use the fact that for every
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}
, the
\infty
-category
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
is canonically tensor-enriched over
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
.
We will denote by

\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})}(-,-)\colon\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})^{\mathrm{op}}\times\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A}

the enriched
\Hom
.
Since
\otimes:\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is cocontinuous in each variable,
3.9
ensures the existence of a tensor product

(-)\otimes_{A}(-)\colon\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\otimes\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\longrightarrow\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})

defined by applying the tensor product objectwise.
If
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
and
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
, we write
M\otimes F
for
\Gamma^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(M)\otimes F
.

Remark 7.14
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
f\colon A\to B
be a morphism in
\mathrm{CAlg}
.
From
6.16
, the adjunction

f^{\ast}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Mod}_{B}\colon f_{\ast}

induces an adjunction

f^{\ast}\circ-\colon\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\leftrightarrows\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{B})\colon f_{\ast}\circ-

To simplifiy notations, for
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
we write
f^{\ast}(F)
instead of
f^{\ast}\circ F
and similarly with
f_{\ast}
.
From
6.16
again, the above adjunction can be described as the adjunction

f^{\ast}\circ-\colon\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\big)\leftrightarrows\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathrm{Mod}_{B}\big)\colon f_{\ast}\circ-\ .

In particular, for every
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
and every
N\in\mathrm{Mod}_{B}
, the canonical map

f_{\ast}(N)\otimes_{A}F\longrightarrow f_{\ast}(N\otimes_{B}f^{\ast}(F))

is an equivalence.

Proposition 7.15
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Assume that
P
is finite and that the strata of
(X,P)
have finitely many connected components.
Then the derived stack
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
admits a global cotangent complex.

Proof.

Let
\Spec(A)
be a derived affine scheme over
R
.
Let

x\colon\Spec(A)\longrightarrow\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)

be a morphism and let
F\in\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\simeq\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathrm{Perf}(A)\big)
be the corresponding
A
-linear family of hyperconstructible hypersheaves.
Let

\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)\coloneqq\Spec(A)\times_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)}\Spec(A)

be the loop stack at
x
.
We view
\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
as a stack over
A\times A
.
Consider the diagonal morphism

\delta_{x}\colon\Spec(A)\longrightarrow\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)

If
d_{0}\in\mathrm{Der}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/R}(A;-)
denotes the
0
-derivation, there are canonical equivalences of functors over
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
:

\mathrm{Der}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/R}(A;-[-1])\simeq\Omega_{d_{0}}\mathrm{Der}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/R}(A;-)\simeq\mathrm{Der}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A}(A;-)

In particular,
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
admits a cotangent complex
x^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/R}
at
x
if and only if the morphism
\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)\to\Spec A\times\Spec A
admits a cotangent complex
\delta_{x}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A}
at
\delta_{x}
.
In this case, one has the relation

x^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/R}\simeq\delta_{x}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A}[-1]\ .

We are thus left to check that the functor

\mathrm{Der}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A}(A,-)\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}

is corepresentable.
Unravelling the definitions, we find for every
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
a natural equivalence

\mathrm{Der}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A}(A,M)\simeq\Map_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})}\big(F,M\otimes_{A}F\big)\ .

Using
5.17
,
6.10
and
[
GHN
, Proposition 5.1]
, we can rewrite the above mapping space as

\int_{x\to y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}\Map_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}(F_{x},M\otimes_{A}F_{y})\ ,

where the integral denotes the limit over
the functor

\mathrm{Tw}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P))\to\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)^{\mathrm{op}}\times\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)\to\mathcal{S}

defined as

x\to y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)\longrightarrow\Map_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}(F_{x},M\otimes_{A}F_{y})

Since the stalks of
F
are dualizable, we can further simplify the above expression as

\displaystyle\int_{x\to y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}\Map_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}(F_{x},M\otimes_{A}F_{y})

\displaystyle\simeq\int_{x\to y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}\Map_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}(F_{x}\otimes F_{y}^{\vee},M)

\displaystyle\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}\left(\int^{x\to y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}F_{x}\otimes F_{y}^{\vee},M\right)\ .

Hence,
\mathrm{Der}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A}(A,-)
is corepresentable and we have

\delta_{x}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A}\simeq\int^{x\to y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}F_{x}\otimes F_{y}^{\vee}\ .

We have to check that the above
A
-module is eventually connective.
For every
x,y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)
, the
A
-module
F_{x}\otimes F_{y}^{\vee}
is perfect.
In particular, there exists an integer
n(x,y)
such that

F_{x}\otimes F_{y}^{\vee}\in(\mathrm{Mod}_{A})_{\geqslant n(x,y)}\ .

On the other hand,
P
is finite and each stratum of
(X,P)
has finitely many connected components.
Since
F
is hyperconstructible on
(X,P)
, we deduce that

N\coloneqq\max_{x,y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}n(x,y)

is a well defined integer.
It follows that the functor

\mathrm{Tw}(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P))^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A}

given by
(x\rightarrow y)\mapsto F_{x}\otimes F_{y}^{\vee}
takes values in
(\mathrm{Mod}_{A})_{\geqslant N}
.
Since
(\mathrm{Mod}_{A})_{\geqslant N}
is closed under arbitrary colimits inside
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
, we finally deduce that

\delta_{x}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A}\in(\mathrm{Mod}_{A})_{\geqslant N}\ .

We are left to check the cotangent complex is global.
In the above situation, let
f\colon\Spec(B)\to\Spec(A)
be morphism and let
y\coloneqq x\circ f
for the induced morphism classifying the
B
-linear family of hyperconstructible hypersheaves
f^{\ast}(F)
.
Then for every
N\in\mathrm{Mod}_{B}
, we have:

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathrm{Mod}_{B}}(\delta_{y}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A},N)

\displaystyle\simeq\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{B})}(f^{\ast}(F),N\otimes f^{\ast}(F))

\displaystyle\simeq\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})}(F,f_{\ast}(N\otimes f^{\ast}(F)))

\displaystyle\simeq\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})}(F,f_{\ast}(N)\otimes F)

by
7.14

\displaystyle\simeq\Hom_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}(\delta_{x}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A},f_{\ast}(N))

\displaystyle\simeq\Hom_{\mathrm{Mod}_{B}}(f^{\ast}\delta_{x}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\Omega_{x}\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)/A\times A},N).

Thus, the conclusion follows from the Yoneda lemma.
∎

7.5.
Representability results

Before giving the proof of
7.8
, let us fix the following couple of notation:

Notation 7.16
.

(1)

For integers
a\leqslant b
, we let
\mathbf{Perf}_{[a,b]}
be the substack of
\mathbf{Perf}
parametrizing perfect complexes of tor-amplitude contained in
[a,b]
.
Notice that
[
SAG
, Proposition 6.1.4.5]
implies that this is an open substack of
\mathbf{Perf}
.

(2)

Given an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, we write
\mathbf{Perf}^{\mathcal{C}}
(resp.
\mathbf{Perf}^{\mathcal{C}}_{[a,b]}
) for the derived stack sending
S\in\mathrm{dAff}
to the maximal
\infty
-groupoid contained in
\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{Perf}(S))
(resp.
\Fun(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{Perf}(S)_{[a,b]})
).

Lemma 7.17
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category with only a finite number of equivalence classes of objects.
Let
a\leqslant b
be integers.
Then the induced morphism of derived stacks

\mathbf{Perf}^{\mathcal{C}}_{[a,b]}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Perf}^{\mathcal{C}}

is representable by an open immersion.

Proof.

Choose a finite set
S
of objects of
\mathcal{C}
representing all equivalence classes of objects in
\mathcal{C}
.
Then the diagram of derived stacks

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Perf}^{\mathcal{C}}_{[a,b]}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{c\in S}\mathbf{Perf}_{[a,b]}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Perf}^{\mathcal{C}}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{c\in S}\mathbf{Perf}\ .}

is cartesian.
Since
S
is finite, the right vertical arrow is an open immersion, as already observed in
7.16
-(1).
Hence, so is the left vertical arrow, whence the conclusion.
∎

Remark 7.18
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is the homotopy type of a topological space
X
in
7.17
, we will note
\mathbf{LC}_{[a,b]}(X)
instead of
\mathbf{Perf}^{\Pi_{\infty}(X)}_{[a,b]}
.

We are now ready to prove the main result of this section:

Proof of
7.8
.

Let
a\leqslant b
be integers and define
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)_{[a,b]}
as
\mathbf{Perf}_{[a,b]}^{\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}
.

7.17
shows that the canonical map

\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)_{[a,b]}\to\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)

is representable by an open immersion.
We now apply Artin-Lurie’s representability theorem to each
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)_{[a,b]}
:

(1)

Truncatedness
: if
A
is discrete, the mapping spaces in
\mathrm{Perf}(A)_{[a,b]}
are
(b-a)
-truncated, and therefore
\mathrm{Perf}(A)_{[a,b]}
is a
(b-a+1)
-truncated
\infty
-category.
Thus, the same goes for
\Fun\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathrm{Perf}(A)_{[a,b]}\big)
, whence the conclusion.

(2)

Locally of finite presentation
: this is the content of
7.11
.

(3)

Integrability
,
Nilcompleteness
and
infinitesimal cohesiveness
: this has been verified in
7.12
.

(4)

Existence of cotangent complex
: this follows from
7.15
.

∎

Corollary 7.19
.

Under the assumptions of
7.8
, let
x\colon\Spec(A)\to\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
be a morphism and let
F\in\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P),\mathrm{Perf}(A))
be the corresponding
A
-linear family of hyperconstructible hypersheaves.
Then

x^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)}\simeq\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})}(F,F)[1]\ .

Proof.

Since
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is a derived geometric stack of finite presentation,
[
SAG
, Proposition 17.4.2.3-(1)]
shows that its cotangent complex at
x
is perfect.
Therefore,

x^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)}\simeq\big(x^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)}\big)^{\vee}\ .

Using the formula obtained in
7.15
, we now obtain:

\displaystyle x^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)}

\displaystyle\simeq\Hom_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}\left(\int^{x\to y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}F_{x}\otimes F_{y}^{\vee}[-1],A\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\int_{x\to y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}F_{x}^{\vee}\otimes F_{y}[1]

\displaystyle\simeq\int_{x\to y\in\Pi_{\infty}^{\Sigma}(X,P)}\Hom_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}(F_{x},F_{y})[1]

\displaystyle\simeq\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})}(F,F)[1]\ ,

where the last equivalence is due to
[
GHN
, Proposition 5.1]
.
∎

7.6.
Perverse sheaves

As corollary of
7.8
, we can obtain a general representability result for the stack of perverse sheaves.
To state the result, let us begin by fixing some notation.

Notation 7.20
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
For every
p\in P
, we can factor the inclusion
j_{p}\colon X_{p}\to X
as

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{p}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\geqslant p}}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\geqslant p}}

where
i_{p}
is a closed immersion and
j_{\geqslant p}
is an open immersion.
Then

i_{p,\ast}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{\geqslant p};\mathcal{E})

commutes with colimits so in particular admits a right adjoint
i_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}
.
We set

j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}\coloneqq i_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ j_{\geqslant p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\ \colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{E})\ .

Lemma 7.21
.

Let
(X,P)
be conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable stable
\infty
-category.
For every
p\in P
, the functor
j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}
restricts to a functor

j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{E})\ .

If furthermore
(X,P)
is locally categorically compact and
P
is finite, then
j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}
is cocontinuous.

Proof.

We can assume without loss of generality that
X=X_{\geqslant p}
.
In this case, we simply write
j\colon X_{p}\hookrightarrow X
for the natural inclusion, and
i\colon X_{>p}\hookrightarrow X
for the inclusion of the open complementary.
For every
F\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
, we have the following fiber sequence:

(7.22)

j_{\ast}j^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow i_{\ast}i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\ ,

computed in
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
Assume now that
F
is
P
-hyperconstructible.
Then the same goes for
i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
, and
6.35
implies that
i_{\ast}i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
is hyperconstructible on
(X,P)
as well.
Thus,
5.20
implies that
j_{\ast}j^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
belongs to
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
In particular,
j^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\simeq j^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}j_{\ast}j^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
belongs to
\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{E})
.

We are left to show that
j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}
commutes with filtered colimits of
P
-hyperconstructible sheaves when
(X,P)
is locally categorically compact.
Under this assumption,
6.42
guarantees that the functor

i_{\ast}\colon\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P_{>p}}(X_{>p};\mathcal{E})\longrightarrow\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})

commutes with filtered colimits.
Since
j_{\ast}
commutes with colimits and is fully faithful, the conclusion follows from observing that the right and the middle terms of (
7.22
) commute with filtered colimits.
∎

Changing coefficients commutes with the exceptional inverse image:

Lemma 7.23
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
be an exact functor between presentable
\infty
-categories.
Assume that the conditions of
5.17
are satisfied.
Then for every
p\in P
, the commutative diagram

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LC}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X_{p};\mathcal{E}^{\prime})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{p,*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{p,*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

is vertically right adjointable.
That is, the Beck-Chevalley transformation

\gamma\colon f\circ j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}\longrightarrow j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}\circ f

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We can without loss of generality assume that
X=X_{\geqslant p}
.
In this case, we simply write
j\colon X_{p}\hookrightarrow X
for the canonical closed immersion and
i\colon X_{>p}\hookrightarrow X
for the inclusion of the open complementary.
Since
j_{\ast}
is fully faithful, it is enough to prove that
j_{\ast}(\gamma)
is an equivalence.
Fix
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
and consider the following commutative diagram whose upper and bottom rows are fiber sequences:

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces fj_{\ast}j^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(F)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces fi_{\ast}i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\ast}fj^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(F)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\ast}fi^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}j^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}f(F)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(F)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\ast}i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}f(F)\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\ast}(\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

The morphisms
\alpha
and
\beta
are tautologically equivalences.
Hence the middle row is a fiber sequence as well.
Finally,
\delta
is an equivalence as recalled in
Section

2.4
.
It follows that
j_{\ast}(\gamma)
is an equivalence as well, whence the conclusion.
∎

Definition 7.24
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable stable
\infty
-category equipped with a
t
-structure
\tau=(\mathcal{E}_{\geqslant 0},\mathcal{E}_{\leqslant 0})
.
Let

\mathfrak{p}\colon P\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}

be a function.
We define

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})_{\geqslant 0}\coloneqq\big\{F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\mid\forall p\in P\ ,\>\pi_{i}(j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))=0\text{ for every }i<\mathfrak{p}(p)\big\}\ ,

and

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})_{\leqslant 0}\coloneqq\big\{F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})\mid\forall p\in P\ ,\>\pi_{i}(j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F))=0\text{ for every }i>\mathfrak{p}(p)\big\}

where the homotopy groups are computed with respect to the naturally induced
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.

It is a standard fact that the above definition gives rise to a
t
-structure
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
on the stable
\infty
-category
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
.
We write
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathcal{E})
for the heart of this
t
-structure.
Following
[
DPSb
]
, we give the following definition:

Definition 7.25
.

Let
S=\Spec(A)\in\mathrm{dAff}_{R}
be a derived affine.
Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function.
We say that an
A
-linear family of hyperconstructible hypersheaves
F\in\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
is
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-flat relative to
S
if for every
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
, one has

M\otimes_{A}F\in{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\ .

Warning 7.26
.

Recall that
A
lies in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
if and only if the canonical morphism
A\to\pi_{0}(A)
is an equivalence, that is when
A
is
underived
. In that case, a
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-flat object automatically lies in
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
.
The converse holds when
A
is a field but is false otherwise.

Construction 7.27
.

Let
i\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
be a closed immersion of topological spaces.
Since the functor
i^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Z;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
is strong monoidal, its right adjoint
i_{\ast}
is automatically lax monoidal.
In particular, for every
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and
F\in\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(Z;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
there is an induced morphism

M\otimes_{A}i_{\ast}(F)\longrightarrow i_{\ast}(M\otimes_{A}F)\ .

Observe that this is an isomorphism for
M=A
.
Since
i
is a closed immersion,
i_{\ast}
commutes with colimits and therefore the above morphism is an equivalence.
In turn, this induces a natural Beck-Chevalley transformation

\alpha_{M,F}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}\colon M\otimes_{A}i^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\longrightarrow i^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(M\otimes_{A}F)\ .

Lemma 7.28
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Assume that
(X,P)
is locally categorically compact and that
P
is finite.
Let
S=\Spec(A)
be a derived affine.
Then for every
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
, every
F\in\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
and every
p\in P
, the natural transformation

\alpha_{M,F}^{!}\colon M\otimes_{A}j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\to j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(M\otimes_{A}F)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

7.21
guarantees that
j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}
commutes with colimits.
Since the transformation
\alpha_{M,F}^{!}
is an equivalence for
M=A
, it follows that it is an equivalence for every
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
.
∎

The following elementary lemma is an immediate consequence of the Tor spectral sequence
[
HA
, 7.2.1.19]
.

Lemma 7.29
.

Let
S=\Spec(A)
be a derived affine.
Let
F\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and assume that
\pi_{i}(F)\simeq 0
for
i\ll 0
.
Let
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then the following are equivalent :

(1)

F
is
k
-connective, that is it belongs to
(\mathrm{Mod}_{A})_{\geqslant k}
;

(2)

for every
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
,
M\otimes_{A}F
is
k
-connective;

(3)

\pi_{0}(A)\otimes_{A}F
is
k
-connective.

The following proposition provides a useful reformulation of
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-flatness.

Proposition 7.30
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Assume that
(X,P)
is locally categorically compact and that
P
is finite.
Let
S=\Spec(A)
be a derived affine.
Let
F\in\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
.
Then the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

F
is
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-flat relative to
S
;

(2)

F
belongs to
\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})_{\geqslant 0}
and for every
p\in P
, the stalks of
j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
have tor-amplitude contained in
(-\infty,\mathfrak{p}(p)]
.

(3)

for every
p\in P
, the stalks of
j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
are
\mathfrak{p}(p)
-connectives and the stalks of
j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
have tor-amplitude contained in
(-\infty,\mathfrak{p}(p)]
.

Proof.

Let
p\in P
.
From
7.29
, the stalks of
j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
are
\mathfrak{p}(p)
-connective if and only if for every
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
, the stalks of

M\otimes_{A}j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\simeq j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}(M\otimes_{A}F)

are
\mathfrak{p}(p)
-connective, where the equivalence follows from the fact that
j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}}
is strongly monoidal.

On the other hand, by definition of tor-amplitude, we see that the stalks of
j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)
have tor-amplitude contained in
(-\infty,\mathfrak{p}(p)]
if and only if for every
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
the stalks of

M\otimes_{A}j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(F)\simeq j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}}(M\otimes_{A}F)

are concentrated in homological degrees
(-\infty,\mathfrak{p}(p)]
, where the equivalence follows from
7.28
.
This finishes the proof of
7.30
.
∎

Remark 7.31
.

Observe that condition
(3)
in
7.30
is stable under base change.
Hence, the
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-flatness condition defines a substack

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
of
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
referred to as the
derived stack of perverse sheaves on
(X,P)
.

Theorem 7.32
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Assume that:

(1)

(X,P)
is of finite stratified type;

(2)

(X,P)
is locally categorically compact.

Then,

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)\longrightarrow\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)

is representable by an open immersion.
In particular,
\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
is locally geometric locally of finite presentation.

Proof.

7.30
ensures that the following diagram of derived stacks

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{p\in P}\mathbf{LC}_{\geqslant\mathfrak{p}(p)}(X_{p})\times\mathbf{LC}_{(-\infty,\mathfrak{p}(p)]}(X_{p})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{p\in P}\mathbf{LC}(X_{p})\times\mathbf{LC}(X_{p})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}},j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}})_{p\in P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j_{p}^{\ast,\mathrm{hyp}},j_{p}^{!,\mathrm{hyp}})_{p\in P}}

is cartesian.
Here
\mathbf{LC}_{\geqslant\mathfrak{p}(p)}(X_{p})
(resp.
\mathbf{LC}_{(-\infty,\mathfrak{p}(p)]}(X_{p})
) denotes the substack of
\mathbf{LC}(X_{p})
parametrizing locally hyperconstant hypersheaves whose stalks are perfect and
\mathfrak{p}(p)
-connective (resp. have tor-amplitude within
(-\infty,\mathfrak{p}(p)]
).
The first part of
7.32
thus follows from
7.16
-(1)
and
7.17
.
We then deduce the representability of
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
from
7.8
.
∎

7.7.
Cohomological Hall algebras

As an application, let us sketch the construction of a cohomological Hall algebra associated to perverse sheaves on a smooth projective curve over
\mathbb{C}
.
From now on, we will assume that
X
is a smooth curve over
\mathbb{C}
equipped with
n
marked points
\{p_{1},p_{2},\ldots,p_{n}\}
.
We see
X
as a stratified space over the poset
P=\{0<1\}
equipped with the middle perversity function, that is

\mathfrak{p}(0)=0\ ,\qquad\mathfrak{p}(1)=-1\ .

Lemma 7.33
.

Let
k
be a field.
Let
(X,P)
as above.
Let
F,G\in{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{k})
.
Then

\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{k})}(F,G)\in\mathrm{Mod}_{k}

is concentrated in homological degrees
[-2,0]
.

Proof.

Let us denote by
\mathbb{D}\colon\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{k})\to\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{k})
the Verdier duality functor.
Then, there is a canonical equivalence

\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{k})}(F,G)\simeq R\Gamma(X,\mathbb{D}(F\otimes\mathbb{D}G))\ .

By Poincaré-Verdier duality, showing that the above complex is concentrated in homological degrees
[-2,0]
amounts to show that
R\Gamma_{c}(X,F\otimes\mathbb{D}G)
is concentrated in homological degrees
[0,2]
.
Since
\mathbb{D}G
is perverse, we are thus left to show that if
F,G\in{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{k})
, then
\Gamma_{c}(X,F\otimes G)
is concentrated in homological degrees
[0,2]
.
By definition
F\otimes G
is concentrated in homological degrees
[0,2]
and both
\pi_{1}(F\otimes G)
and
\pi_{0}(F\otimes G)
are punctually supported.
Since
X
is a curve over
\mathbb{C}
, the only potential non zero terms of the spectral sequence

E^{2}_{p,q}\colon\pi_{p}\Gamma_{c}(X,\pi_{q}(F\otimes G))\Longrightarrow\pi_{p+q}\Gamma_{c}(X,F\otimes G)

are thus

E^{2}_{0,0},\ E^{2}_{0,1},\ E^{2}_{0,2},\ E^{2}_{-1,2},\ E^{2}_{-2,2}\ .

This implies the sought-after range. ∎

Following
[
PS
, §4.1]
, there is a canonical simplicial object

\mathcal{S}_{\bullet}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)\colon\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{dSt}_{R}\ ,

which satisfies the
2
-Segal condition.
Here
\mathcal{S}_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)\simeq\Spec(R)
is the final object in
\mathrm{dSt}_{R}
, while

\mathcal{S}_{1}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)\simeq{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)

is the derived stack of
\mathfrak{p}
-perverse sheaves we just constructed.
Moreover,

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{ext}}(X)\coloneqq\mathcal{S}_{2}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)

can be explicitly described as the derived stack sending a derived affine
S=\Spec(A)
to the
\infty
-groupoid consisting of fiber sequences in
\Cons^{\mathrm{hyp}}_{P,\omega}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
of the form

F_{1}\longrightarrow F_{2}\longrightarrow F_{3}\ ,

where we further ask that
F_{1}
,
F_{2}
and
F_{3}
are
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-flat relative to
S
.
The simplicial structure of
\mathcal{S}_{\bullet}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
induces a canonical correspondence

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{ext}}(X)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)\times{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

where
p
sends a fiber sequence as above to its extremes
(F_{3},F_{1})
and
q
sends it to its middle term
F_{2}
.
We have:

Lemma 7.34
.

(1)

The map
q
is representable by proper algebraic spaces.

(2)

The map
p
is derived lci.

Proof.

We start by point (1).
Notice that this question is insensitive to the derived structure, so we will pass to the truncations.
Direct inspection reveals that
q
is
0
-truncated; since source and target are Artin stacks, it follows that
q
is representable by algebraic spaces.
Observe now that if
X
is a smooth (possibly open)
\mathbb{C}
-analytic curve equipped with a trivial stratification, then the statement is known (see e.g. the proof of
[
PS
, Theorem 4.9]
).
Since the collection of representable maps by proper algebraic spaces is closed under fiber products, we can reduce ourselves to the case where
X
is an
\mathbb{C}
-analytic disk
\mathrm{D}
, equipped with the stratification over
P=\{0<1\}
whose closed stratum consists exactly with the origin of
\mathrm{D}
.
Observe that
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(\mathrm{D})
can be also described in terms of representations of the following quiver
Q
:
0
1

More specifically, the stable
\infty
-category
\mathsf{Rep}(Q)
of
\mathbb{C}
-linear
Q
-representations, but equip it with the
t
-structure
\tau
defined by declaring that an object
F\in\mathsf{Rep}(Q)
is connective if and only if

F(0)\in(\mathrm{Mod}_{k})_{\geqslant 0}\qquad\text{and}\qquad F(1)\in(\mathrm{Mod}_{k})_{\geqslant 2}\ .

Then
[
DPSb
]
defines a derived moduli stack
\mathbf{Rep}(Q,\tau)
parametrizing
\tau
-flat families of finite dimensional
Q
-representations.
Explicitly,
\mathbf{Rep}(Q,\tau)
sends a derived affine
S=\Spec(A)
to the
\infty
-groupoid of
A
-linear
Q
-representations
F
such that both
F(0)
and
F(1)[-2]
are projective
A
-modules of finite rank.
Inspection reveals that
\mathbf{Rep}(Q,\tau)
and
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(\mathrm{D})
are equivalent.
We now use the valuative criterion of properness
[
Stacks
, Tag 0A40]
to verify that
q
is proper.
Let therefore
A
be a discrete valuation ring with fraction field
K
and consider the lifting problem

\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(K)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{ext}}(\mathrm{D})}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(A)}
\displaystyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(\mathrm{D})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

let
F
be the
A
-linear family of perverse sheaves classified by
s
, and let

F^{\prime}\longrightarrow F_{K}\longrightarrow F^{\prime\prime}

be the extension of
K
-linear families of perverse sheaves classified by
t
, where
F_{K}\coloneqq K\otimes_{A}F
.
Write furthermore

M\coloneqq F(0)\qquad\text{and}\qquad N\coloneqq F(1)\ .

Notice that we can review this extension as an extension of perverse sheaves with coefficients in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
(whose stalks will no longer be perfect).
Define
\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime}
to be the image of
F\to F_{K}\to F^{\prime\prime}
, computed in the abelian category
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
.
By construction,
\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime}=(\widetilde{M}^{\prime\prime},\widetilde{N}^{\prime\prime},\widetilde{\alpha}^{\prime\prime},\widetilde{\beta}^{\prime\prime})
is a subobject of
F^{\prime\prime}=(M^{\prime\prime},N^{\prime\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime\prime})
.
This means that
\widetilde{M}^{\prime\prime}
and
\widetilde{N}^{\prime\prime}
are subobjects of
M^{\prime\prime}
and
N^{\prime\prime}
, and hence they are torsion-free.
Since
A
is a valuation ring, they are automatically free
[
Stacks
, Tag 0539]
.
Besides,
M\to\widetilde{M}^{\prime\prime}
and
N\to\widetilde{N}^{\prime\prime}
are surjective, and hence
\widetilde{M}^{\prime\prime}
and
\widetilde{N}^{\prime\prime}
have finite rank.
Thus,
\widetilde{F}^{\prime}\coloneqq\ker(F\to\widetilde{F}^{\prime\prime})
is flat and of finite rank as well.
This provides a solution of the previous lifting problem.
The uniqueness follows at once from the uniqueness of the epi-mono factorization for the morphism
F\to F_{K}\to F^{\prime\prime}
.
Thus,
q
is proper.

We now prove point (2).
The computation of the relative cotangent complex for the map
p
done in
[
PS
, Proposition 3.6]
, reduces us to check that for every field
k
and every pair
(F_{3},F_{1})
of
k
-linear families of perverse sheaves, the object

\Hom_{\Cons_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X;\mathrm{Mod}_{k})}(F_{3},F_{1})\in\mathrm{Mod}_{k}

is concentrated in homological degrees
[-2,0]
.
This follows from
7.33
.
∎

At this point, following the discussion in
[
PS
, §4.2]
, we are led to:

Corollary 7.35
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective curve over
\mathbb{C}
equipped with an algebraic stratification over
P=\{0<1\}
.
Let
\mathfrak{p}
be the middle perversity function.
Then
\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{b}}({}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X))
carries a canonical
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-monoidal structure of Hall type.
Similarly, the Borel-Moore homology and
G
-theory of
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
carry Hall multiplications.

Remark 7.36
.

These (categorical, cohomological,
K
-theoretical) Hall algebras generalize the Betti Hall algebras of
[
PS
,
M
]
(a.k.a. the Hall algebras of the character variety), which in turn can be seen as a generalization of Schiffmann-Vasserot CoHA of the commuting variety
[
SV
]
.
Exploiting the microlocal nature of perverse sheaves, it is natural to expect that these “perverse CoHAs” admit a presentation as a Jacobi algebra, generalizing the one of
[
M
]
.
Moreover, following
[
DPSa
]
it is natural to wonder whether there exists a torsion pair on
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{Perv}_{P}^{\mathrm{hyp}}(X)
giving rise to a “perverse geometric Yangian”.
We will come back to these questions in a future work.
